# GrapiX — Build Plan

*The EffectCraft-core application, in backend and frontend modules. Rewritten 18 November 2026; every step keeps its ID.*

GrapiX is a broadcast motion-graphics application in three processes. A **Render Engine** owns time and Program pixels. An **Editor** is After Effects-style authoring: a Scene Manager of independent graphics, layers, per-property keyframes, effects and presets, scripts and plug-ins, and Figma and PSD ingest. **Playout** is the operator's application: cue, take, continue, out, clear, and an as-run record. An **MCP surface** lets agents operate and author through the same commands a person uses, and never take or clear Program.

The core is EffectCraft's. The render path is a GrapiX-owned, GPU-first compositor (ADR-0003) built from EffectCraft's WGSL kernels, blend formulas, layer-style and shading algorithms, path operators and importers, each ported with attribution under the step that owns it. The editing model follows EffectCraft and After Effects: an Effects & Presets tree of 306 catalogued effects (the panel counts how many render), layer styles, text animators, keyframe tracks and curves, After Effects scripts, ScriptUI panels and plug-ins. Windows only for now.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | In execution |
| **Date** | 9 September 2026; rewritten around the EffectCraft core and split into backend and frontend modules on 18 November 2026 |
| **Tracker** | `progress.html` (the page) and `progress.json` (the committed state): the only place a step's status lives |
| **Rule** | A step does not start until every step it needs is done. The `Needs` column decides this, not the module. |

Tracks: `CON` Contracts · `PLAT` Platform · `BE` Backend · `UI` Interface · `RES` Research · `EXT` External gate · `AI` Gen-AI

**293 steps in 27 modules: 14 backend (177 steps), 7 frontend (72 steps) and 6 parked (44 steps).**

## How this plan is organised

- **Backend and frontend are separate.** Backend modules (B1 to B14) are everything that runs without a window: the Rust engine and services, the contracts, platform, transport, MCP, the object and text libraries, and the import and scripting runtimes. Frontend modules (F1 to F7) are the two Tauri apps' interfaces: the Editor's workspace, panels, animation, effects, import and script panels and preview monitor, and the Playout application. A capability and the panel that surfaces it are separate steps in separate modules (the effects engine B5 and its panels F4; import B11 and F5; scripting B12 and F5; parity B13 and F7).
- **Step IDs are stable.** The numbers (3.22, 11.36, 18.2) are cited in code, ADRs, evidence folders and `memory.md`, so none was renumbered. The module ids are new. Steps whose number starts with 11 are spread over many modules because the old "Editor UI" phase held 80 steps that are really several different things.
- **A module id is a reading order and a priority guide, not a gate.** Backend modules come first because the interface is built against an API that is already proven, and a frontend module needs the backend module that feeds it; but a few steps need a step in a later module (3.15 needs 7.1, for example), and the `Needs` column is the truth.
- **Parked is not removed.** Modules X1 to X6 keep their steps, dependencies and Done-when text, out of the main order, so any of them can be pulled back in. Removed steps are gone: the old parallel tracks (P.1 to P.3) and the old UI research phase (9.1 to 9.7) on 18 Nov 2026, and 4.5 and 16.2 on 8 Nov.
- **Status is not written here.** Done, in progress and not started live in the tracker, and a step is marked done only after its whole Done-when has been verified by execution (LLM-GOVERNANCE.md).
- **Old phase numbers.** Done-when text written before this rewrite still says "Phase 3", "Phase 11", "Phase 15" and so on, in the old numbering. Old Phase 0 and 1 are B1 (0.14 is F1); 2 is B2 (2.27 is F6); 3 is B3 and B5; 4 is B4; 5 is B6; 6 and 7 are B7 (7.2 and 7.3 are B11); 8 is B8; 10 is B11; 11 is split over B5, B9, B10, B12 and F1 to F7; 12 is F6 (12.10 and 12.11 are B7); 13 is B13 (13.5 is F7); 14 is X1; 15 is X2; 16 is B11, B12, B14 and X5 (16.15 is F5); 17 is X3; 18 is B11 (18.10 is F5); 19 is X4 (19.3 and 19.7 are X6).

**Design reconciliation, 24 November 2026.** Rows 1.13, 1.14, 3.32, 6.10, 8.17, 11.119 to 11.152, 12.13 to 12.22, 16.24, 16.25 and 19.11 to 19.16 come from the design review and the screens built in Figma (cross-check in docs/design/review/build-plan-vs-screens-2026-11-24.md). All are pending. Where they differ from an earlier row, the owner decides which wording stands, and the older row is reworded only then: keyframes only and no template animations (11.131 against 11.23, 11.25, 11.46, 11.70, 11.76); Scene ID and Cue ID (1.13 against 6.1, 8.10, 12.2); layers beyond the base layer (12.13 against 12.10); the Data Hub as a fourth application (19.14 against invariant 1 and 19.3); library folders (11.122 against 11.43); formats per graphic (11.123 against 7.8); the blend mode count (the Editor shows the 38 EffectCraft modes; confirm against 3.20). UI names in the design (Cue, Preload, Stack, Playline, Airview, Element, Style, Effects, Phase, Scene Bank, Inputs, Data Hub) are working titles until 11.127 is done.

## Decisions in force

**15 October 2026 (user): the product.** GrapiX combines After Effects-style composition authoring with a Scene Manager and live broadcast playout like XPression and Viz. Authoring covers layers, editable properties, keyframes, curves, effects and composition previews. The Scene Manager groups multiple independent graphics in one saved container. Playout cues, takes, continues and clears published graphics; the Render Engine owns the frame clock and the production pixels sent to outputs, including DeckLink SDI (X2). Editor, Playout and Render Engine keep their existing authority boundaries. SDI readiness needs the SDK, hardware and timing tests in X2; discovering a card does not satisfy those gates.

**15 October 2026 (user): authoring follow-up.** Properties expose inline value edits and visible keyframe controls, with values evaluated at the playhead and edits to animated properties keyed there. The Scene Manager shows a preview for each graphic; current thumbnails reuse the existing viewport and say when they are partial, and engine-backed preview and Program parity stay with 13.5 and 13.6. These belong to 11.9, 11.21, 11.63 and 11.64 and need desktop interaction verification.

**15 October 2026 (user): no design-system concept in authoring.** The Scene Manager is one saved container of independent graphics, each with its own layers, timeline and undo history (11.64), like compositions inside an .aep. The design-system steps (10.1 to 10.5) and design-system-only parts of earlier steps are historical compatibility, not requirements for creating, saving or browsing graphics. No new token-ingest, brand-pack confirmation or drift workflows. Existing saved metadata stays readable and is not deleted.

**11 to 13 October 2026 (user): the render core.** The main render path is GPU-first and GrapiX-owned. Whole-core adoption of EffectCraft's render and gpu crates was halted: upstream rasterises text, shapes and masks on the CPU by design, its GPU suite fails on DX12, and 4K misses the 50p frame period even for two solid layers (ADR-0003). EffectCraft stays the preferred component source. Steps 3.15 to 3.22 own the GPU compositor, and no done step is evidence that GrapiX draws on the GPU unless it says so. Colour follows the project: compositing happens in the project's declared working colour space, not unconditionally in linear light (3.15).

**16 October 2026 (user): UX and Playout workflow reviews.** From `docs/proposals/GrapiX-Editor-UX-Direction-Review.md` (Q1 to Q8) and `GrapiX-Broadcast-Playout-Workflow-Review.md`: the engine preview comes before further Editor polish, so 11.41 and then 11.65 (Q2); explicit save is the only project writer, with crash-recovery snapshots kept apart (Q3); on-air Update touches only fields marked liveUpdate (default false) and Continue advances a stop point (Q4); operator pages live in Playout, with the Editor keeping a Test fill (Q5); the glossary is Project, Graphic, Layer with a "Composition" viewer, and Take, Page and Playout library on the operator side (Q6); 17.2 uses project variables (Q7); the first release targets sports and esports with one Program channel (Q8). The research phase of Q1 was removed on 18 Nov 2026.

**8 and 19 November 2026 (user): Windows only.** macOS and every other OS are out of scope: the plan, the CI and installer jobs and the Tauri bundle targets build for Windows only. 4.5 (Windows versus macOS pixel equality) and 16.2 (VideoToolbox decode) were removed on 8 Nov. On 19 Nov every step that still named another OS was reworded to Windows: 0.3, 2.2, 2.5, 2.8, 3.1, 3.3, 4.2, 5.1, 11.1, 11.2, 11.24, 12.1, 14.5 and the Editor 2.0 acceptance bullet. The macOS installer job, the dmg bundle target and the Apple signing steps were removed. Other-OS builds return as new steps when the user re-adds them.

**8 November 2026 (user): the plan review.** From the two-classifier review (`docs/evidence/plan-review-2026-11`): 5.7, control-port scope enforcement at the transport, is done before the control port is exposed beyond loopback (X1); 5.5's done-when was made executable; a done-when that cannot be executed as written is rewritten to an executable form, and approved by the user, before its module opens (the one-line intents in the parked modules wait for that); the order stood.

**8 November 2026 (user, on Jev's recommendation): the 4.4 threshold for a GPU render.** Unchanged: no channel more than one code value apart and at most 0.1% of pixels differing. Comparing a GPU render with the CPU reference of the same frame, a differing pixel does not count toward the 0.1% if it is a proven tie flip (the GPU bytes are the CPU encode of its own working-space floats, within 1e-4 relative of the reference's, and the reference lay on a rounding tie); the one-code limit still applies to every pixel and the strict count is recorded beside the tie-aware one. Applies to 3.17 to 3.22 and later GPU steps.

**18 November 2026 (user): effects and presets are the main thing.** The Editor lists EffectCraft's 306 effects, its 8 original presets and GrapiX's own broadcast effects, with categories as collapsible dropdowns, and supports After Effects scripts, ScriptUI panels and plug-ins the way After Effects does (backend B5 and B12, frontend F4 and F5). Panels are draggable and resizable like After Effects, using XPression's module knowledge and layout (F1). This outranks polish work.

**18 November 2026 (user): Figma, telemetry, load, brand.** Figma is consumed through an approved agent's MCP with no personal access token and no keychain entry in GrapiX (18.2). Live telemetry is a Playout feature only; the Editor has none (2.28 cancelled), and Help sits beside Window in the Editor. A GPU and CPU load governor keeps a spike from making live output hiccup (3.24). GrapiX is "Graphics by SKWAD" under the SKWAD design system, both apps dark (SKWAD live mode), with the name always written GrapiX.

**18 November 2026 (user): scope cut and module split.** Removed the old parallel tracks (P.1 procurement of the GPU, DeckLink and AJA hardware, NDI SDK access and lab time, the I/O configuration step; P.2 licensing decisions; P.3 UI research kickoff) and the old UI research phase (9.1 to 9.7). 15.1, 16.1 and 18.1 carry no dependency now. The written licence positions stay in `docs/p2-licensing-positions.md`; a few documents still say "P.1" or "P.2" and are history, not requirements. The rewrite on the same day parked the remote engine, the output server and genlock, the Gen-AI template factory, the Data Server, and generative and spatial content as X1 to X6, and split the plan and the tracker into backend and frontend modules.

**Standing prohibitions, all modules (2 October 2026).** No neural frame generation or temporal AI upscaling on Program; nothing generative runs at Take time; agents never Cue, Take, Continue, Clear or configure outputs (invariants 60 to 66). Per-scene JavaScript runs only inside 16.4's deterministic sandbox, and no author- or agent-scoped tool reaches playout control.

## Backend modules

Everything that runs without a window. The Rust engine and services own the truth; the interface only asks for it.

### Module B1 — Foundations and contracts

*Backend.* One Cargo workspace and lockfile, pinned toolchains, Windows CI, the refusal, rate, ID and revision types, and the contracts every other layer reads: scene, objects, materials, colour, assets, packages, protocol v3 and capability, with three mock peers under a conformance suite. Rust is the source of truth and TypeScript is generated. Blocks everything.

> **Milestone.** Three products can be built in parallel from here.

### Module B2 — Platform and diagnostics

*Backend.* No UI. Atomic writes, filename and path rules, the project and asset stores, fonts, tokens and roles, one structured log for every process with correlation ids and a diagnostic bundle, and the bind policy, wire version and transport bounds every listener shares. These fail quietly if rushed.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `2.1` | Atomic write helper: unique temp, fsync, retry on contention, directory fsync | PLAT | 0.1 | Both known rename races closed; one implementation only |

| `2.22` | Transport bounds: timeouts, connection caps, acknowledgement eviction, low-latency accepted streams, and a flush signal for stop | BE | 2.21 | Every listener sets read and write timeouts, caps its connections and bounds a frame before authentication; the control server evicts acknowledgements it can no longer be asked to repeat; accepted control streams are low-latency; an asset transfer is bounded in total as it arrives, not after; a stalled peer is dropped, shown by a test for each plane (control, asset, MCP HTTP); the stop path leaves when the transport has flushed the reply, not after a 250 ms sleep. From the OSI layering audit of 17 Nov 2026 (`docs/audit/osi-layering-2026-11-17.md`); proposed by Claude when the user asked for its serious findings to be fixed. (findings 5, 6, 11.) (Done 18 Nov 2026, on named readings: `docs/evidence/2.22`. The control plane has **deadlines, not an idle timeout** (Jev, 1.00): 10 s from accept to the first request, 10 s for a frame that has begun, 10 s per write; an authenticated peer that is merely quiet is left alone because the shells keep a connection open for the life of the window and the protocol has no ping. A cap of 32 connections (asset 16, MCP HTTP 16), 4 KiB before authentication, the latest 64 acknowledgements kept, `TCP_NODELAY` on every accepted stream (my decision: Jev gave 0.32), and the stop path waits for the transport's one-shot with a 3 s limit instead of sleeping 250 ms (the real engine was gone 98 ms after its reply). The asset plane and the MCP HTTP server verify no credential, so for them "before authentication" is the first-frame or header deadline and the cap; an asset transfer is bounded as it arrives (65,536 chunks, 64 KiB a chunk, 4 GiB a connection) and an offence ends the connection with the bound named in the engine's log, not a refusal reply (no new refusal code, no protocol bump). **Not bounded, and named:** an MCP request body that stalls after complete headers; any MCP write; every read and write on a Windows named pipe; a transfer is still held in memory up to its declared chunks. 30 of 30 mutations fail a named check (A5 only after its test was fixed); gate on the final tree: fmt and clippy -D warnings clean, `npm run check` exit 0 (76 Node, 424 Editor, 1055 Rust with 38 ignored, conformance 631/0/9).) |

| `2.24` | Asset plane: authentication, resume, retry, and transport errors named as transport errors | BE | 2.21 | A publish or a fetch over the asset plane carries a credential the engine verifies when a token is configured (required off loopback); a transfer cut part way resumes from its last whole chunk and the client retries with backoff, each shown against a killed connection in a test; a concurrent duplicate upload is answered idempotently; a reset or an end of stream is reported as a transport error, not as a protocol failure. A decision for the user on the credential. From the OSI layering audit of 17 Nov 2026 (`docs/audit/osi-layering-2026-11-17.md`); proposed by Claude when the user asked for its serious findings to be fixed. Slice 1 (the credential): the asset plane verifies the control plane's gx1 credential on every connection's first frame when the engine has a token, and then follows `--bind`; refusals by name in each reply type; `gx-credential mint`. Slices 2 to 4: a transfer belongs to its content hash, so a cut one resumes from its last whole chunk (the receiver keeps up to 16 unfinished transfers; the client redials with doubling backoff, default four tries, and asks where it stands); a duplicate chunk or upload, concurrent or repeated, is answered and counted once (a changed chunk is refused by name); a reset or an end of stream is `Transport` and a malformed reply is `Protocol`, and the Editor's publish reads a cut connection as unreachable, not as a protocol failure. | `2.28` | Settings service: user, machine and project levels | PLAT | 2.1, 2.4, 2.6 | One typed settings store with two files and a rule for the third. **User settings** follow a person (language, confirmations, what a new animation starts with, what the app does at start). **Machine settings** belong to this computer (GPU adapter, caches, ports, process priority, log level). **Project settings** (anything that changes how a graphic looks when rendered) stay inside the project file and never in settings, so changing a setting can never change a graphic on air. Defaults, a version number, atomic writes with a backup copy kept at each change, change events, a clear refusal for an unknown or out-of-range value, a reset to defaults, and a damaged file set aside (never lost) with the backup or defaults used. Built so far: the service, its tests and the Editor commands; the apps read their settings from here row by row (11.81 onwards). Research: docs/evidence/xpression-benchmark/research.md. |
| `2.29` | Workspace store: layouts as versioned files | PLAT | 2.28 | The layouts of each application are written as versioned files in the user's own folder, not in the browser's storage: the shipped ones, the saved ones, the session copy and the last good copy. A write is atomic; a failed write keeps the layout in memory and says so; a file from a newer version is left alone; a file that cannot be read is set aside, never overwritten. It follows the pattern of the settings service (2.28). **Built so far (23 Nov 2026):** `Shared/workspace-store` (gx-workspace-store): per application, saved copies roaming and session, last good and the one in use on this computer; safe ids, an envelope check and a 1 MiB limit, refused by name (`workspaceInvalid`); atomic writes keeping the readable copy they replace; a file that does not read set aside and never copied over the backup; a newer build's file never written over, deleted or set aside (`workspaceNewer`); Editor shell commands `cmd_workspace_*`. 25 store and 4 shell tests, both refusals driven by the conformance audit. Open: the page writing its layouts through it (11.95, 11.96), so the browser's storage is no longer used. Evidence: docs/evidence/11.94/. |

### Module B3 — Render core: the GPU compositor

*Backend.* Headless, and the heart of the application (ADR-0003). GrapiX owns a GPU-first compositor: layers, transforms, 2D drawing, text, masks and blend modes on the GPU, composited in the project's declared colour space, with Program and Preview output that quantises once and reads back asynchronously within a bound, and a load governor so Program is never late because something else spiked. EffectCraft is the component source (WGSL kernels, blend formulas, layer-style and shading algorithms, path operators, importers), ported with attribution under the step that owns each; whole-core adoption was halted on 12 Oct 2026. The acceptance rules are in the standing notes.

| `3.15` | Project working colour space: compositing follows the project's declared colour space | CON | 3.3, 7.1 | `WorkingColorSpace` offers sRGB (display-referred) and linear sRGB, with no default. A project declares one — new projects sRGB; a project created before 3.15, with no declaration, reads as linear sRGB, the space it was authored in — and an unreadable declaration refuses by name. Every scene carries its project's space (scene schema 3; a schema 1 or 2 scene upgrades losslessly to linear sRGB, proven against a witness saved by the schema-2 build), and reading or writing a scene whose space differs from its project's refuses by name. The engine converts tagged sources into the scene's space once, composites there, and applies the sRGB Preview or Rec.709/BT.1886 Program transform once: a rendered 50% black-over-white overlap gives each space's independently computed reference value to ±1 code value at both outputs, and the reference operations for anti-aliased coverage, gradient interpolation and blur averaging, which 3.16, 3.17 and 3.22 must match, are tested in both spaces. The pixel-gate baselines and the colour ramp are unchanged; ADR-0002 B.1 and the contract docs state the rule **Extended 22 Nov 2026 (user: the colour space shall support 8, 16 and 32 bit): `ColorDepth` (8, 16 or 32 bits per channel, After Effects style) sits beside the working space. A project declares it (new projects 16; a file saved before depth reads as 32 bit float, which is what it rendered in), a scene carries its project's (schema 4; an older or journalled scene without it reads as 32), and a scene in another depth than its project refuses `SceneColorDepthMismatch`. At 8 and 16 bit every layer's blend result and every effect pass's output is stored as a whole level held to 0-1 (round half up); at 32 bit it stays a float and an over-bright value survives. The CPU reference rounds in `blend::store_pixel`, the GPU twin in `composite.wgsl` and `effects.wgsl` (the level count rides in a spare uniform slot), and the parameter block grew from 32 to 64 floats for it and for wider effects. New Project offers the depth. Verified: CPU rounding test, a hardware test at all three depths (over-bright matches exactly; at worst one level apart, on under 1% of components, where the GPU's half-precision source lands on the other side of a rounding step), refusal audit, shell test. **Shown in the Editor, 22 Nov 2026:** the opened project now carries its working space and depth (`OpenedProject`, read from its settings, also for the project already open), and the line under the Scene Manager's title reads, for example, `Project Finals · 16 bpc · sRGB · <path>` (32 reads `32 bpc float`); tested in the shell (the dialog's depth, and an old folder reading as linear 32 bit) and in the web tests; not yet looked at in the real desktop window. ### Module B4 — Pixel gate

*Backend.* The instrument, built before anything that could change pixels so there is a baseline to compare against: reference scenes, a headless harness on Windows, committed baselines, and a CI gate with a diff threshold that fails the build.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `4.1` | Reference scene set: colour ramp, safe areas, text, vector, 3D, every rate family | BE | 3.13 | Covers each supported fit and blend mode |
| `4.2` | Headless render harness on Windows | PLAT | 4.1 | Runs in CI without a display |
| `4.3` | Baseline capture, committed to the repo | BE | 4.2 | Reference images versioned |
| `4.4` | CI gate with a diff threshold that fails the build | PLAT | 4.3 | A deliberate one-pixel change is caught |

> **Milestone.** There is now an instrument. Nothing after this changes pixels unmeasured.

### Module B5 — Effects engine: effects, layer styles, text animators

*Backend.* The main thing (user, 18 Nov 2026), in the engine. The typed effect registry and the broadcast effect set on the GPU, EffectCraft's 306-effect catalogue ported in broadcast-first batches, layer styles and text animators. Each effect is a typed contract with a GPU kernel and a CPU reference within the 4.4 threshold, and every parameter is keyframable. None may silently drop an option it cannot render: a missing capability is a named finding. The panels that surface them are module F4.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `3.22` | Typed effect registry and the broadcast effect set on the GPU | BE | 3.16, 3.20 | Effects are a contract enum with typed parameters, generated TS and JSON Schema, and no string-keyed parameters; an unknown effect refuses by name. First set: Gaussian, box and directional blur, glow, drop shadow, fill, tint, gradient ramp, levels, curves, hue/saturation, brightness/contrast, exposure, and linear, radial and iris wipes, as GPU kernels ported from EffectCraft with attribution; each matches a CPU reference within the 4.4 threshold on DX12 and Vulkan, and every parameter is keyframable |
| `3.25` | Port EffectCraft's effects beyond the first 16, broadcast-first | BE | 3.22 | Effects from EffectCraft's catalogue are ported in priority order for broadcast graphics (Transition, Stylize, Distort, Generate, Blur & Sharpen, Color Correction, Keying, then the rest; Audio and analysis effects last), each as a typed contract effect with a GPU kernel and a CPU reference within the 4.4 threshold on DX12 and Vulkan, every parameter keyframable, attributed per ADR-0003; the panel's "not yet ported" count falls with each batch and is the row's measure. From the user's direction of 18 Nov 2026 ("effects and presets shall be the main thing"; "support AE scripts or other extensions and plugins as After Effects does"); proposed by Claude. **Batch 1 done, 18 Nov 2026; the row stays open** (`docs/evidence/3.25`). Ported from EffectCraft: **Invert** (all, red, green or blue, with a blend back to the original), **Posterize** (2 to 255 levels), **Threshold** (Rec. 709 luminance at or above the level is white) and **Vibrance** (saturation weighted `(1-s)*min(s,0.5)`: greys and fully saturated colours are left alone, half-saturated ones gain most; not "dull colours gain more", which a first test assumed and the formula refutes). Each is a typed contract effect with one parameter table, a CPU reference and a WGSL kernel (ids 19 to 22) reading the same packed block; posterize rounds half up on both sides because WGSL `round` goes to even. The panel now lists **20 available, 286 not yet ported** (was 16 and 290). Tests: contract 283, engine 199 plus the hardware GPU parity test (7 new cases, default hardware backend, within the 2e-4 gate) and the engine-level GPU render test; 16 mutations each failed a named test (one survived first, a vibrance weight tested only at s = 0.5, and a case at s = 0.8 now catches it). Gate: fmt 0, clippy 0, `npm run check` 0. **Open:** the 4.4 threshold on Vulkan was not run (default hardware backend only); batches 2 onward. **Batch 2 done, 19 Nov 2026:** **Tritone** (shadow, midtone and highlight colours on luminance, blend back to the original), **Channel Mixer** (a weighted sum of red, green and blue per output channel, a constant, monochrome), **Color Balance** (shadows, midtones and highlights shifted by luminance, preserve luminosity) and **Gamma/Pedestal/Gain** (per channel, with Black Stretch), as contract effects with CPU kernels and WGSL kernels (ids 23 to 26) on the same packed block; 24 of 306 now render (282 not yet ported). Tests: contract 284, engine 202 with the hardware GPU parity test (9 new cases, within the 2e-4 gate); 11 mutants each failed a named test (one survived first, the Color Balance midtone weight, because no test pixel sat between mid grey and white). Gate: fmt, clippy and `npm run check` 0. On "the existing stagger presets are expressed through them": the Editor's Stagger (row 11.77) offsets selected keys of several elements and is not a text animator, so what is expressed through animators here is EffectCraft's text-animator preset set; whether more is meant is the user's to say. |

### Module B6 — Transport

*Backend.* Location-transparent from the first line: the control, asset and media planes, session, auth and reconnect, locality in the capability exchange, scope enforcement at the transport, and the L0 asset handoff. Local is an optimisation, not another code path.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `5.1` | Control plane over a named pipe | BE | 1.9 | Ordered, acknowledged, sequenced |
| `5.2` | Session, auth, epoch, reconnect and reconciliation | BE | 2.9 | Program survives a control disconnect |
| `5.3` | Asset plane local: hash preflight, transfer only the difference | BE | 2.7 | Re-publish moves no unchanged bytes |
| `5.4` | Media plane local: shared memory or handle passing | BE | 3.11 | Frames reach a consumer with no copy |
| `5.5` | Locality declared in the capability exchange | BE | 1.10 | A conformance test reads `EngineCapability.locality` over the wire from the real local worker and from a LAN mock served over the loopback transport, and every consumer's media profile comes from `gx_media_plane::negotiate` applied to that declared value (through `profile_for`, which refuses a codec the engine does not offer by name); no consumer branches on locality (boundary rule R9); the engine's offered codecs agree with it. (Reworded twice on 8 Nov 2026, user decisions: from "Consumers adapt without code change", which could not be executed, and then from "Playout chooses its asset path by it", which assumed a Playout asset path that does not exist; the asset-path half is `5.8`.) |
| `5.6` | Conformance suite passes over the real transport, not just mocks | BE | 1.12 | Same suite, both paths |
| `5.7` | Control-port scope enforcement: every request checked against the token role's scopes at the transport, before the engine sees it (added 8 Nov 2026 from the done-row review: `server.rs` verified a token's signature and then ignored its claims; only the MCP server enforced scopes) | BE | 5.2, 8.13 | A conformance test sends every control request with an author-scoped token and each is refused by name at the transport; the engine handler is never reached (asserted by trace). Done before Phase 14 exposes the port beyond loopback. |
| `5.9` | Published assets reach the engine: the 5.3 transfer wired into the engine's asset plane and the Editor's publish (added 8 Nov 2026, user decision; before it no published font or image could reach the real engine) | BE | 5.3 | The Editor's publish sends every asset the scene declares (re-hash checked from its store) through preflight and the 5.3 transfer on the same asset-plane address before the scene, and the real engine keeps the verified bytes; a conformance test against the real worker shows a scene with a font and an image asset refused AssetMissing alone, its assets transferred, then published and taken to Program, an image object refused by name until 3.17 draws images, and a re-publish moving no bytes. (8 Nov 2026, `5.8` review: the bytes it delivers were not kept for restore; fixed by `5.11`.) |
| `5.8` | L0 asset handoff by local path (ADR-001's co-located asset plane) (added 8 Nov 2026, user decision, split from `5.5`) | BE | 5.5, 5.9 | A co-located engine accepts a published package by local path, re-hashing every asset before use and refusing by name a path outside the package directory or a hash mismatch; the publisher chooses path or chunked upload from the declared locality through one negotiation function; a conformance test publishes the same package both ways and the engine prepares identical scenes. (Done 8 Nov 2026, user decision: a file swapped for a link between the directory check and the open is not re-checked on the open handle; every byte is still verified before use. Noted and tracked as hardening; evidence `docs/evidence/5.8`.) (8 Nov 2026 review: 13 confirmed findings, 12 fixed in 5.8's code, including a crafted package that aborted the engine; the 13th, from 5.9, is `5.11`; `docs/evidence/5.8/review-2026-11-08.md`.) |
| `5.11` | Engine restores asset-bearing scenes after a forced kill (added 8 Nov 2026, user decision, from the `5.8` review: since `5.9` a journaled scene with assets could not be rebuilt, so a killed engine would not start) | BE | 3.12, 5.9 | Every verified asset the engine accepts, by chunked upload or by path, is written to an engine-owned content-addressed store beside its recovery journal before the publication is acknowledged. On restore each journaled scene's assets are read back from that store and re-hashed before preparation. A scene whose bytes are missing or fail their hash is dropped with a named reason, and the engine still starts output-inhibited; it never refuses to start for it. A test kills a real worker after publishing the Inter lower third by each route, restarts it with the same journal, and takes the restored revision (Taken on the T0 workstation, a named skip below T0). A second run, with one stored asset removed and another altered, starts, names both dropped scenes and their reasons, and still serves the scene whose bytes are intact. (Done 8 Nov 2026: store `<journal>.assets` written by the asset port outside the engine lock; restore re-reads and re-hashes every declared asset; drops named in `<journal>.dropped.json` and on stderr, refused by reason, revision floor journaled; conformance against four real workers killed and restarted; evidence `docs/evidence/5.11`.) |
| `5.12` | Re-publish a take while an older revision of it is on Program or cued (added 8 Nov 2026, user decision, found while verifying `5.11`: the journal check refused the publish and inhibited output) | BE | 3.12, 5.11 | The recovery journal records a prepared scene for every revision Program or the cue names as well as each take's latest published revision, and its check accepts Program or the cue on any journaled revision of a published take, so publishing is additive on air as everywhere else (invariant 30). A test on a real worker publishes a take, takes it, and publishes an edit of it while revision 1 is on Program: the edit is Published as revision 2, Program keeps emitting revision 1 and is never inhibited, and revision 2 can be cued and taken. After a forced kill and restart both revisions are restored and Program is revision 1. `npm run check` passes. (Done 15 Nov 2026: the journal records every revision Program or the cue names as well as each take's latest; its check accepts them and nothing else; restore judges a take's revisions as one; a real worker publishes, takes, publishes an edit while revision 1 is on Program and keeps both across a forced kill; evidence `docs/evidence/5.12`.) |
| `5.10` | Editor publishes by the engine's declared route: by path when co-located, chunked over a LAN (added 8 Nov 2026, user decision, split from `5.8`; reworded 8 Nov 2026, user decision: the Editor cannot read the control-plane capability exchange, because R6 bars a control client and 5.7 answers the control port only for Playout, so it reads the same declaration from the engine on its asset plane) | BE | 5.8, 5.9, 5.11, 5.12 | The Editor's publish (`cmd_publish` → `gx_editor::publish::publish_document` → `gx_asset_plane::publish_as_package`) learns the engine's declared locality only from the engine itself. It sends a closed `{locality:{}}` read to the asset-plane address it publishes to, and the engine answers from the same field it declares as `EngineCapability.locality`. The Editor holds no control-plane client (R6) and names no `Locality` (R9). The publish always writes one verified package, named by content hash and never by user text, into its package folder: `GX_EDITOR_PACKAGE_DIR` if set, else `platform::package_exchange_root()`. The launcher and `gx-engine-host` give a co-located engine that same folder as `--package-dir`. The package is published through `gx_asset_plane::publish_package`: co-located by path with 0 asset bytes, over a LAN chunked with only the missing bytes. It is removed after the reply. A LAN-declared engine reads no package path, and `gx-render-worker` refuses `--lan --package-dir`. Nothing about the directory goes on the wire or into `EngineCapability`. A conformance suite runs against two real workers (one with `--package-dir D`, one with `--lan`) and both mocks. It shows each peer's asset-plane answer equal to the `EngineCapability.locality` read over the control plane. It publishes the Inter lower third from an Editor `AssetStore` through the functions `cmd_publish` calls, with folder D, to each worker: `LocalPath` with 0 bytes and the package inside D, and `ChunkedUpload` with exactly the font's bytes. Both publications are Published with the document's content hash, D is empty afterwards, and the harness takes each with its pinned hash: Taken on the T0 workstation, a named skip below T0. The suite also shows each of these refused by name as `PublishRefused`, never `Unreachable`: a folder outside D, a co-located worker without `--package-dir` (`PackageDirectoryNotConfigured`, no fallback), and a path offered to a LAN-declared engine that holds a directory. An asset with no content hash is refused before any socket opens. Mutations of the declared value, of the peer's answer and of the server rule each fail a check. `npm run check` passes with check:boundaries clean. (Done 15 Nov 2026: `GX_EDITOR_PACKAGE_DIR` or `<cache root>/packages` is the Editor's package folder; the engine answers a closed `{locality:{}}` read on the asset plane from the field its capability declares; `gx_asset_plane::publish_as_package` writes, publishes and removes one package; a LAN-declared engine reads no path; `gx-render-worker` refuses `--lan` with `--package-dir`; conformance against three real workers and both mocks, 9 mutations; not run: the live check in the Tauri window; evidence `docs/evidence/5.10`.) |

### Module B7 — Playout and Editor services

*Backend.* The two headless services, fully testable before any window exists: Playout (published library and stable Take IDs, take intents, rundown, outputs, overlay layers, the as-run record) and Editor (project and scene CRUD, validation, package build and publish, preflight).

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `6.1` | Published library store; stable scene keys | BE | 1.8 | The library holds the scene key and the content hash, stable across republish. A Cue ID is not stored here: it is typed in Playout's show (1.13, 12.13). The wire field `takeId` carries the scene key until the protocol is versioned. |
| `6.2` | Cue, take, continue, out, clear as intent; committed frame returned | BE | 5.1 | No caller can specify wall-clock time |
| `6.3` | Revision pinning; refusal on mismatch | BE | 0.8 | Stale revision cannot go to air |
| `6.4` | Rundown persistence and sequence engine | BE | 6.1 | Cursor survives restart |
| `6.5` | Output configuration; is_live gated on tier, platform and reference | BE | 3.2 | Live refused below T0, naming the failing condition |
| `6.6` | Automation trigger evaluation; execution explicitly deferred | BE | 6.4 | Deferral is a named refusal, not silence |
| `7.1` | Project and scene CRUD | BE | 2.6 | Refuses writes until a project folder exists |
| `7.4` | Scene validator: every unsupported case refused by name | BE | 3.13 | Shares one enum with the engine |
| `7.5` | Design system enforcement: three severities plus override log (historical compatibility; not required for authoring or publish) | BE | 1.5 | Overrides recorded with a reason |
| `7.6` | Package build, verify, publish; design-system version stamped when present (optional, historical) | BE | 1.8 | Publishing is all-or-nothing |
| `7.7` | Preflight | BE | 7.4 | Returns structured refusals with allowed values |
| `12.10` | Overlay layers with channel/layer targeting on one Program channel | BE | 1.9, 6.2 | Cue, Take, Update, Continue, Out and Clear carry a `channel` and `layer` target; only the Program channel is accepted and any other channel refuses by name; a bug, a lower third and a ticker on separate layers are cued, taken, updated and cleared independently on one output with deterministic z-order and a named refusal on conflict; a targeted clear removes only its layer when the expected page still matches (decision Q8) **Slice 1 built 20 Nov 2026; the row stays open** (`docs/evidence/12.10`): cue, take, clear and phase carry a channel and layer target (`Envelope.target`; none is Program/main); another channel is refused `ChannelNotSupported` and another layer `LayerNotSupported`, by name, before the engine sees the command; the client keeps its target across a redial; the as-run record names the layer. 8 transport tests, the refusal audit, 11 mutants (10 caught, one equivalent). **Slice 2 built 24 Nov 2026** (Panchayati topic 13, proposal 36). Program targets now carry signed integer layers from -99,999 through +99,999. An absent target and the legacy spelling main resolve to layer 0. The transport refuses out-of-range layers as `LayerNotSupported` and passes valid targets to the peer. Single-layer peers explicitly refuse nonzero targets. Verified by 10 TCP and wire tests, 6 caught mutants and `npm run codegen:check`. **Engine cue slice built 24 Nov 2026** (Panchayati topic 14). The worker stores and restores distinct nonzero layer cues. Program status adds an optional per-layer list and Take acknowledgements allow a delayed incoming start frame. Wire tests cover both additions and the worker currently reports layer zero. **Layers slice built 24 to 25 Nov 2026** (task 112, taken over from codex by claude when its limit ran out; `docs/evidence/12.10`). The engine now holds one Program scene per layer and draws them together, lowest layer first, so a higher layer is in front. A new GPU `LayerStack` (`compositor/stack.rs`, `stack.wgsl`) puts each scene over the one below it in the working space, rounds to the project colour depth at every step (not once at the end) and encodes once; every scene keeps its own tiles. A take on an empty layer is on air at once. A take on an occupied layer plays the old scene's authored Out first and starts the new scene on the frame the Out ends (reported `Leaving` and `Queued` in the per-layer status), or cuts when the old scene has no Out; a later take on the same layer takes the place of the queued one and the leaving scene keeps leaving on its own clock. Clear and Phase act on one layer; a Clear with no target is layer zero only and drops what was queued behind that layer; Stop is refused while any layer is on air. A scene of another canvas size is refused by name (`LayerCanvasMismatch`) and changes nothing. Layer scenes and queued replacements are journaled and restored (an older journal reads with none; an orphan replacement, an unpublished scene or a layer out of range refuses recovery by name). `program` in the status is still layer zero. **Verified:** 253 worker library tests; all 56 hardware tests on both backends (`GX_GPU_316_BACKEND=vulkan` and `dx12`, run with `--ignored`, they are never run by a plain `cargo test`), among them 7 stack tests (order, one scene is that scene, three layers, depth held at every step with an exact 8-bit alpha, a scene with foreign tiles refused) and 8 engine tests (layers on air together in order, Out then replace, no Out is a cut, a later take replaces the queued one, Clear and Phase per layer, Clear drops the queue, another canvas refused, one scene on two layers drawn at each layer's own time); contracts 366, control-plane 77, transport 65, editor 157, mock engine 18, Playout 13, Playout MCP 23, conformance 8 with the refusal audit; `codegen:check` 563 files current (the new refusal regenerated), `check:boundaries`, `check:protocol`. **Mutations: 15 of 15 caught** (an occupied layer ignoring the Out, a replacement one frame late, layers unsorted, a clear leaving the queue, a legacy clear clearing every layer, another canvas accepted, a later take restarting the Out, a leaving scene reported on air, a restore forgetting layers, a lone layer not drawn, Stop allowed with only a layer, every layer drawn at the first layer's time, the shader putting the lower scene over the upper, the shader skipping the depth, the lowest scene reading a lower that is not there). One survived the first time: skipping the per-step depth rounding was invisible because the test allowed one level of difference, so a new test compares the alpha byte exactly (34, 41 and 20 of 255 make 85 when rounded at every step and 84 when rounded once) and now kills it. Two earlier mutant runs were stopped by hand because a test process hung; they were re-run with a 15 minute limit and ended caught by an assertion. **Targeted clear built 25 Nov 2026** (decision Q8; Jev A, A, A at 0.98, 0.98, 1.0, Laya A, A, C at 0.01 to 0.07, `docs/evidence/12.10/jev-laya-clear.json`): `ClearRequest` carries an optional `expected: {takeId, revision}` (omitted when none, so an older sender's Clear is unchanged; it lost `Copy`). A Clear that names its page removes the layer's scene only if that is the take and revision on the layer; otherwise it refuses `ClearExpectationUnmet {layer, expected, onAir}` (onAir is "nothing" for an empty layer), changes nothing and leaves what was queued alone. While a scene leaves with a replacement queued, an expectation of the leaving scene holds until the replacement starts and one of the replacement only after; clearing the leaving scene drops the queue. The check is made when the command arrives; a Clear at a later frame is not rechecked at its frame. 3 tests (the wire form and two engine tests), 9 mutants caught, the refusal listed as a conformance limitation (the mock engine has no layers); no caller sets `expected` yet. **Playout sends a layer, 25 Nov 2026** (owner: yes, send a layer): the transport client sends any request on a layer (`Client::handle_on_layer`; layer zero as no target, so a main-layer command stays readable by an older receiver, any other layer as an explicit numeric target; the target the client keeps for later requests is put back), and the Playout operator path has layer-aware calls: the shell's `cue_on`, `take_on`, `clear_on` (with the expected page) and `phase_on` and the Tauri commands `cmd_cue`, `cmd_take`, `cmd_clear`, `cmd_phase` take an optional `layer` (absent is layer zero; `cmd_clear` also an optional `expected`), and the service's `cue_on`, `take_on`, `clear_on`; the operator record names the layer. A one-layer engine refuses another layer by name and nothing lands on layer zero. 2 transport, 1 shell and 1 service test. The Playout MCP has no program command to give a layer: agents never cue (invariant 65), by design. No screen picks a layer yet. **Open:** Update, Continue and Out as commands of their own; clear all (armed, 12.14); a transition set per layer or per take (decision C-5); the Editor and the Playout screens (P7 Stacks, Figma 50:750) choosing a layer, and a layer for a scene's default (12.13); mixed-version enablement of explicit numeric targets (an older version-8 receiver rejects a numeric layer); a layer scene that needs the engine to hold tiles for every scene at once, which is not measured for memory yet. **Playout's Stacks panel built 25 Nov 2026 (see 12.13):** every Transport command now goes to the stack in a Stack field (zero as no layer) and the page draws the engine's per-stack status; this supersedes "No screen picks a layer yet" above. The Editor's scene default stack is still open. |

| `6.7` | Remote control: commands from other systems over TCP, UDP and HTTP | BE | 5.7, 6.2, 2.21, 2.23 | Other systems (station automation, control panels, scripts) can drive Playout over three channels of our own design, all doing the same jobs: load, cue, take, out, clear one layer, clear all, update a field, run a run-order item, next, previous. **TCP**: one text command per line; every command gets a coded reply (2xx done, 4xx the caller's mistake, 5xx the engine's fault) with an optional request number; changes of state (what is on air, layer by layer) are pushed to everyone listening, which is also the on-air signal. **UDP**: one command per datagram, for simple triggers where a lost packet does no harm; there is no connection to log in on, so every datagram carries a token and a rising counter and an old or repeated one is dropped; a size limit; replies and state go back to the sender; never the only way to take something to air. **HTTP**: the same jobs as a documented JSON interface. All three are off, or on this computer only, until switched on; each needs a credential; a role per connection (view, cue, take) is checked by the 5.7 scopes; every take is on the as-run record (12.11) with who, from where and when; one controller at a time can hold control. Replaces the deferral in 6.6 for station automation. Not in this row: vendor protocols such as RossTalk (proprietary); notes on how RossTalk works are in docs/evidence/xpression-benchmark/rosstalk-notes.md, and a compatible adapter can be added beside these three later if wanted. Research: docs/evidence/xpression-benchmark/research.md. |

### Module B8 — Automation: MCP and AI-native authoring

*Backend.* Before the interface, on purpose: the whole domain is operable and automatable headless, and the interface is built against an API that is already proven. Tools are generated from the contracts, scope-gated and revision-checked, tiered (read, author on draft handles, propose), with AI-native authoring and a supervising agent on top. Agents never cue, take, continue, clear or configure outputs.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |

| `8.2` | system.capability — supported enums, tier, versions, build hash | BE | 1.10 | An agent never has to guess |
| `8.3` | project, scenes, objects | BE | 7.1 | Full property surface reachable |
| `8.4` | materials, assets, fonts | BE | 7.1 | Including relink and usage lookup |
| `8.5` | animation, including apply_preset | BE | 1.6 | Presets parameterised, never authored by the caller |
| `8.6` | bindings | BE | 1.1 | Data schema discoverable |
| `8.7` | designsystem.* (historical compatibility) and motion.* | BE | 1.6 | Import and commit are admin-scoped |
| `8.8` | validate.* | BE | 7.7 | Refusals machine-parseable |
| `8.9` | render.preview returning a real frame | BE | 3.11 | An agent can see its own output |

| `8.14` | Tiered MCP surface: read, author on draft handles, propose | BE | 8.13, 11.36, 11.38 | Three tiers. **Read:** scenes, assets, bindings, tokens and engine status. **Author:** 11.36 commands only, on a stateless draft handle. **Propose:** submit a changeset to Playout's approval queue (13.7). Targets the MCP 2026-07-28 specification, verified against the official spec and the Rust SDK before coding. A conformance test enumerates every MCP tool and asserts that none reachable by an author- or agent-scoped token can Cue, Take, Continue, Clear or configure outputs, while 8.11's operator-scoped tools stay behind their scopes (invariant 65). An author tool cannot modify a published revision; an unknown schema version is refused (the server fails closed) **Every-tool scope conformance built 25 Nov 2026** (claude-grapix-f1, `docs/evidence/8.14`; verification only: no contract, wire, generated or service change): `conformance/src/mcp_scope.rs`, nine checks in the conformance run, assemble every handler the MCP crates provide (the shipped author-only Editor server and every Playout tool) with the Program tools on a mock engine that records every request it gets. "Changes Program or outputs" is the control plane's own exhaustive `ClientRequest::required_scope()` (Continue is a `Phase`, which needs Take), not a list of names. Proved by running the tools: an agent credential (no scope, Author, Report, both) calling an operator-scoped tool is refused by name and the engine hears nothing, with arguments valid enough to reach it; no tool an agent credential can reach sends the engine a Program-changing request (including a valid `render.preview`, which sends a Render request only); each operator-scoped tool is opened by its own scope, which the recorder sees reach the engine (the positive control), and by no other operator scope; a deliberately hostile tool is caught (the negative control); the Editor MCP crate and the Editor service have no direct dependency on the control plane or Playout; the operator scopes and the scope each of the seven operator tools needs are written out again so the classification cannot change with the table. **Verified:** `cargo test -p gx-conformance --lib mcp_scope` 3 passed; the project's conformance command exit 0, CONFORMANT, 653 passed, 0 failed, 9 skipped (644 before: exactly these nine checks); 16 of 16 mutants caught, including the gate letting every caller through, `playout.take` and `outputs.configure` needing only an author scope, and a cue-only token able to clear (the first run found two weaknesses in the suite itself, fixed). **Findings, not fixed (other rows' calls; written into the suite as ratchets):** `objects.list`, `assets.list` and `fonts.list` are declared in the tool table and have no handler anywhere, while 8.3 and 8.4 are marked done; and `tools/list` is not filtered by the caller's scopes, so an author credential sees the admin-scoped `designsystem.import` and `designsystem.commit` it can never call (refused by name when called). **Slice 2 built 25 Nov 2026** (claude-grapix-f1, the same evidence folder; three of the five gaps of the review of 25 Nov; again no contract, wire, generated or service change): three more checks in the same suite. No tool that writes under the Author scope can name a published revision (no Take ID or content hash among the inputs of the tools `tools/list` shows; the operator's own tools must show them, so a blind reader fails). A published revision is byte-identical after every author write: package rebuilt, same take published again (a new revision with another hash), a publish naming revision 1, a stored scene of the same id edited and deleted; and review under a Take scope moves the review and nothing else. A scene whose schema version this build does not know (5, 99, 0) is refused `SceneSchemaUnsupported` with both versions by every tool that reads a scene and by `scenes.edit`, the stored file is never rewritten, and a package is not built from one. `docs/evidence/8.14/spec-check.md` sets the 2026-07-28 specification's words beside the real HTTP transport by running it (`services/mcp-server/tests/http/spec_probe.rs`): the unsupported revision (-32022 with the supported list), a header that disagrees with the body, no sessions, 405 on GET and DELETE all answer as the specification says; and it says plainly that **this is the check made after the fact, not the pre-coding one the row asks for**, and that the newest-`rmcp`-release half is open (the fetch tool was refused github.com, crates.io and docs.rs; the pinned 3.9.0 was read from the local registry and knows 2026-07-28). **Verified:** conformance 656 passed, 0 failed, 9 skipped (653 before: these three checks); `gx-mcp-server` 24 + 9 passed; 16 of 16 mutants caught across Playout's library, the contract's tool table, the scene reader and the package reader, all restored byte for byte. **Findings, not fixed:** S1 every refusal is a JSON-RPC -32000 error and never a tool result with `isError: true`, where the specification puts tool execution errors (pinned for an unknown tool); S2 two revisions are claimed, the SDK knows five (a 2025-11-25 client is answered 2025-06-18); S3 insufficient scope is HTTP 200, not 403; S4 the credential is not the specification's OAuth profile (2.23's decision); D1 a scene of an unknown version can be deleted (pinned); L1 `packages.publish` and the Playout tools each own a library and nothing joins them, so a published revision cannot yet be reviewed or taken through MCP. **Not built:** the three tiers (Read, Author on a draft handle, Propose) and the approval queue 13.7; a read-only credential does not exist (`Scope::Author` covers reads and writes), so the Figma E4 "Reader" role cannot be represented. |

> **Milestone.** The application is fully operable and automatable with no interface at all.

### Module B9 — Editor core: commands, revisions and the template contract

*Backend.* The foundations the editor modules build on, cheap now and expensive to retrofit: the typed command model, undo on the command log, hashed scene revisions with review state, asset provenance, the component registry and project variables, the headless render entry point, the lint framework, named animation phases and stop points, and the template control contract with its field constraints.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `11.20` | Editable animation contract and service integration | CON | 1.6, 7.1, 8.12 | Document existing timeline/preset consumers and extend only missing scene channels, easing and validated edit operations; Rust/TS round-trip keys, evaluate rational-rate frames consistently, preserve old scenes and undo/revision semantics without widening preset-only MCP authoring |
| `11.25` | Named animation phases on the scene timeline | CON | 11.20, 11.22 | The scene timeline carries named `in`, `hold`, `out`, `continue` and `update` phases as frame spans over the existing channels, matching the `MOTION_PHASES` vocabulary presets already use; phases round-trip Rust/TS, are authored and previewed independently in the Editor, refuse overlap and out-of-range spans by name, and a scene authored before phases existed still parses unchanged |
| `11.26` | Template control contract: declared operator fields | CON | 11.18, 11.19 | A scene declares which object properties an operator may fill, as a typed, named field list carried on the document; each field names its target object/property, its type and its default, refuses an unknown or non-fillable target by name, round-trips Rust/TS, and is authored in the Editor without widening the preset-only MCP authoring surface |
| `11.36` | Typed editor command model: every authoring mutation is a shared command | CON | 1.12, 7.1 | Every mutation of authoring content is a variant of one command type defined in `Shared/`, with TS generated and a JSON Schema (2020-12) generated beside it for every command; the codegen staleness check covers the schemas. Every mutation surface — the Editor shell's commands, the editor MCP tools, and project-api (4100) if it is built (no server binds 4100 today) — accepts only that type, and no surface accepts a client-supplied whole document. A boundary-guard test fails a build that adds a mutation path outside it, and a conformance test shows the Editor's mock Playout peer receives no command that alters Program or outputs (invariant 60) |
| `11.37` | Undo/redo on the command log | BE | 11.36 | Project-level and per-module undo (Ctrl+Z) and redo (Ctrl+Shift+Z) are built on the 11.36 command log, not on per-module state snapshots. Applying N commands to a fixture scene then undoing all N gives a content hash equal to the original; redoing all N gives the post-edit hash exactly; per-module undo affects only commands tagged with that module, shown by a test with interleaved commands from two modules. 11.29's per-scene isolation and 11.9's one-transaction multi-select still hold |
| `11.38` | Immutable, content-hashed scene revisions with review state | CON | 11.36, 6.3, 7.6 | Publishing creates an immutable revision identified by a content hash, carrying a state of `draft`, `proposed` or `approved` that only a human sets. Publishing the same content twice gives the same hash; changing one property changes it. Playout loads revisions by pinned hash only (invariant 61): the Playout mock refuses an unknown or unpinned hash by name (`RevisionMismatch` or a new named code), the real Playout service refuses identically, and the conformance suite confirms both. Invariant 30's additive, all-or-nothing publish still holds (8 Nov 2026: re-publishing a take while an older revision of it was on Program was refused by the journal check and inhibited output; fixed by `5.12`.) |
| `11.39` | Asset provenance and licence fields | CON | 11.36, 2.7 | Every asset record in a `.gpx` project carries origin (`human`, `tool` or `model`), tool or model name and version, provider, licence class, optional prompt hash, source references, approving user and timestamp, and an optional C2PA manifest reference; the fields may be empty for human-made assets, but the schema exists. The `.gpx` schema version is bumped and a migration test upgrades a pre-11.39 project losslessly; save and reload preserve every provenance field byte-for-byte; importing through the asset manager fills `origin` and the tool name automatically |
| `11.41` | Headless render entry point in the Render Engine | BE | 11.38, 3.13, 5.6 | One control request renders revision X at frame N to an image; the golden-frame gate and 8.9's `render.preview` use it, and thumbnails (11.65), approval previews (13.7) and Phase 13 parity use it when they land. The Program-timing criterion moved to 3.21 (user decision, 16 Oct 2026): the software Program path cannot hold cadence, so it cannot be shown here |
| `11.40` | Component registry and project variables | CON | 11.36, 11.41 | Named project variables (colour, font, spacing, motion duration and easing) are referenced by name from objects instead of copied; a registry holds reusable components (scorebug, lower third, player card, bracket, …) with typed slots. Changing a variable updates every bound object, shown by 11.41 headless renders of a fixture scene before and after; deleting a variable still referenced is refused by name; components and variables round-trip through `.gpx` save/load. Design-system tokens are not required (15 Oct amendment) |
| `11.42` | Lint framework and first rules | BE | 11.36, 11.41 | A lint command returns structured findings: severity, rule, object and fix hint. First rules: title/action-safe violation; missing glyph for any bound field or target language; text overflow against a worst-case string fixture (longest player names, CJK, Arabic RTL); required template field left unbound. Each rule has one failing and one passing fixture, run as tests. A missing glyph is a refusal-class finding, never resolved by a silent font fallback (invariant 66). Refusal-class findings block publish (7.6) and preflight (7.7); the rest warn. Findings use the 0.6 refusal structure (condition, cause, fix), so 11.58 renders them unchanged |
| `11.53` | Timeline: named stop points and a looping hold | CON | 11.22, 11.25, 11.36, 11.38 | Extends 11.25's phases, which already give In (`in`), Out (`out`) and the held state (`hold`). A hold phase may be marked looping, and named stop points tell Playout's Continue where to pause. Moving or retiming a key is one 11.36 command; values evaluated at sampled frames match the expected curve exactly in rational time; stop points and the loop flag are carried in the published revision and the Playout mock reads them; Continue on the on-air revision advances to the next named stop point exactly once and never loads a new revision (decision Q4) |
| `11.54` | Template field constraints | CON | 11.26, 11.27, 11.38 | Extends 11.26's declared fields with a range, enum, max length and fallback rule per field; Playout forms, data binding (19.7, 19.8) and the template factory (17.4) fill them. The field schema is part of the published revision; a value outside a field's type, range, enum or length is refused by name by both the Playout mock and real Playout; a fallback rule never produces different pixels without a declared state (invariant 18). Each field also carries `liveUpdate`, default false: Update on air accepts only eligible fields and refuses an ineligible one by name with pixels unchanged; eligibility grants no authority, which stays with operator scope and engine-time intent (decision Q4) |

### Module B11 — Import and design sources

*Backend.* Figma is the primary source, read through an approved agent's Figma MCP with no token in GrapiX, and the source is retained beside the template. PSD, AI, After Effects and Lottie importers, the import report that names every substitution, fidelity gates against the source's own render, and token ingest kept for historical compatibility. Anything the engine cannot draw is refused or substituted by name, never approximated silently. The import panels are module F5.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `7.2` | Import pipeline: four tiers, compatibility report, source retained | BE | 1.1 | Every substitution named in the report |
| `7.3` | Import fidelity gate: source reference render versus imported render | BE | 4.3 | Diff stored with the template |
| `10.1` | Token ingest from W3C Design Tokens JSON | BE | 1.5 | Round-trips without loss |
| `10.2` | Token ingest from Figma and Claude Design | BE | 10.1 | Variables and published styles mapped |
| `10.3` | Unexpressible report at ingest | BE | 10.1 | Names what the source held and tokens cannot |
| `10.4` | Versioned, immutable store with provenance | BE | 10.1 | Human confirmation before v1 |
| `10.5` | Drift report across published templates | BE | 7.6 | Lists every template behind the current version |
| `11.59` | Importers on 2.0 objects: PSD/AI and After Effects | BE | 7.2, 11.39, 11.41, 11.47, 11.48, 11.50, 11.51, 11.53 | PSD/AI and After Effects importers rebuilt on the 2.0 objects (Figma is Phase 18). After Effects precomps import as live scenes, not flattened clips; every imported asset is stamped with an 11.39 provenance record. A fixture file per importer imports and renders with every asset exact in 11.41 golden-frame tests; any unsupported node type produces a named finding, never a silent drop |
| `16.5` | Lottie import path | BE | 7.2 | Reduces the Adobe dependency |

| `18.2` | Figma ingest through the Figma MCP, with the source retained and no token in GrapiX | BE | 7.2, 11.39, 8.1 | **Decided by the user, 18 Nov 2026:** Figma works the way it does in Claude and Codex, signed in through the MCP, with no personal access token and no keychain entry in GrapiX. Figma's remote MCP server (`mcp.figma.com`) admits only clients on its catalog (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, VS Code and a few more; others are refused at client registration, and a personal access token or a self-made OAuth app does not open it: Figma forum threads and write-ups, May 2026; not checked against Figma's own docs), so GrapiX does not sign in to it itself. The design is **agent-brokered**: the user's own approved agent reads the frame through its Figma MCP (`get_metadata`, `get_design_context`, `get_screenshot`, variables and image fills) and hands it to GrapiX through GrapiX's Editor MCP tool (8.x scopes; authoring only, never Program). A second path to **verify** before relying on it: the Figma desktop app's local MCP server (no OAuth, the user's open Figma session), which GrapiX could read as an MCP client. Either way the raw response is stored content-hashed beside the template, so a re-import can be diffed and an offline re-run needs no network; no token or sign-in is ever stored in a scene, package or log; every imported asset carries an 11.39 provenance record; all layers, layer styles and variables map or are named as findings. Replaces the earlier REST design (a file key and a token in the OS keychain), which is dropped. **Registration built in part, 20 Nov 2026:** `npm run mcp:register -- --root <project-store>` (`tools/register-mcp.mjs`) writes the agent's MCP config (the project's `.mcp.json` by default) with `grapix` (the Editor MCP over stdio, no secret) and `figma-desktop` (`http://127.0.0.1:3845/mcp`); it keeps every other server, refuses to overwrite a differing entry without `--force`, supports `--dry-run` and `--remove`, and refuses a non-local Figma address. **The second path is verified:** Figma's desktop MCP server answered an unauthenticated `initialize` on this machine as "Figma Dev Mode MCP Server", so it is not behind the remote server's client allowlist. **The Figma link module, built in part, 20 Nov 2026 (user: a module, not a middleware):** `services/figma-link` (`gx-figma-link`) is GrapiX's own MCP client of that local server: loopback only (a non-local address is refused before a socket opens), the MCP handshake and session id, `tools/list` and `tools/call`, every reply kept as received with its SHA-256 (so a re-import can be diffed), bounded reads and timeouts, no new dependency. Fake-server tests (6 integration, 5 unit) cover the handshake, session carrying, an event-stream reply, errors, a silent server and an unreachable one. **Against the real Figma desktop app** the handshake works ("Figma Dev Mode MCP Server"); `tools/list` was left unanswered by the app, with plain PowerShell too, so what the app needs for it (a design file open, a prompt, a setting) is **not yet found**, and `tools/call` is untested live. Figma's server has no way to register GrapiX's MCP in it, so "GrapiX MCP into Figma" is not possible; the agent side is the registration tool above. **Typed like Claude's own use of Figma's MCP (user, 20 Nov: "it shall act like how Claude treats Figma MCP"):** a Figma URL is parsed to file key (branch key for a branch URL) and node id (`node-id=1-2` is node `1:2`; only https figma.com links, a bare `1:2` means the open file's selection); `get_metadata`, `get_design_context`, `get_screenshot` and `get_variable_defs` are typed calls; a reply is read as content blocks (text, image, unknown kept as sent); a result flagged `isError` is an error carrying its text; nothing in a reply is run or obeyed, it is data. 20 tests now (12 unit, 7 integration, 1 live ignored). **The ingest tool, built in part, 22 Nov 2026 (user: "Build ingest tool"):** the Editor MCP tool `scenes.ingest_figma` (`EditorCommand::ImportFigma`, index 14, in the witness set; `services/editor/src/figma.rs`) takes the text of Figma's `get_metadata` as the user's agent read it and stores one scene: the frame is the canvas (its width and height, 18.3), its nodes are objects at their Figma positions (the metadata's coordinates are relative to the root frame, checked on a real reply from the Figma desktop app), nesting is kept as groups with child ids in Figma's order, `hidden` stays hidden, and the metadata text is retained in the project by content hash. It holds no token and makes no network call, so it works for any LLM that can call both MCPs (Claude, Codex, Gemini). The report names, per node, what `get_metadata` does not carry: fills, strokes and effects, corner radii, text content, fonts, clipping and auto-layout; text and pixel leaves become empty boxes at the right place (tier flattened) and a node with no box is reported dropped. A page list, several frames, a cut-short reply or an unusable size refuses and stores nothing. 11 unit tests and 3 tests through the shipped `gx-editor-mcp`; an existing id refuses. **Full-fidelity route, built in part, 22 Nov 2026 (user: it should be practically Figma imported into a real-time motion graphics tool like After Effects with EffectCraft):** `scenes.ingest_figma` now takes a typed `frame` (`gx_contracts::figma_frame::FigmaNode`: kind, box relative to the root, rotation, opacity, visibility, blend mode, solid, linear and radial paints, strokes with weight and align, corner radius or four radii, effects, text with characters, family, style, size, line height, letter spacing, align, case and decoration, children) instead of, never with, `metadata`; the agent reads the frame through Figma's MCP and writes it into that shape, so any LLM can do it and GrapiX stays deterministic. Mapped: fills and gradients to the object's paint, strokes, radius, opacity, rotation (Figma counter-clockwise to the scene's clockwise, same top-left pivot), blend mode, visibility, text as text objects with size, line height, tracking in thousandths of an em, alignment and case, a frame's own paint as a rectangle behind its children, the root's fill as the bottom object. Every family and style becomes one font declared **missing** (never a system font), and the scene passes the engine's strict reader. Named on the report per node, never silent: effects (waits for 11.60 layer styles), picture fills, vector outlines (bounding box stands in), per-corner radii, clip content (11.47), inner and outer stroke alignment, stacked paints, justified text. A misspelt field refuses. 10 unit tests and 2 tests through the shipped binary on top of the 11 and 3 for the metadata route. **Not yet shown on real data:** Figma's desktop server refuses `get_design_context` asset writes until a folder is added in Figma Dev Mode, MCP panel, Allowed directories. Still open for 18.2: text content, fills, fonts and radii (needs `get_design_context` read into the scene: 18.4 and 18.5), image fills and vectors as assets with 11.39 provenance, instances as components (18.6), variables (18.7), the retained design context and screenshot beside the metadata, and the live `tools/list` and wiring the link into the Editor service and its status in the UI, the content-hashed store beside the template, the ingest and the findings. **Pictures and fonts follow the project layout above (user, 22 Nov 2026):** the Figma pictures are PNG or WebP files in `Images/<frame>/` of the project, and the ingest resolves `image` references by name against that folder; the fonts come from `Fonts/`. **Pictures and fonts built, 22 Nov 2026:** a picture fill names a file in the project (`image`: the file name without its extension, with Figma's scale mode `fill`, `fit`, `crop` or `tile`) and the ingest looks it up in `Images/<frame>/` (the folder is the root frame's name, or `imageFolder`), `.png` then `.webp`; found, it is an image object with the matching fit and the scene's library names the file; a vector or instance that arrived as its picture is tier rasterized; not found, the report names the node and the exact path to file it at. A text node's font is looked up in `Fonts/` by family and style: found, it is ready and the scene's library names the file; not found, it stays declared missing and is named. A frame's picture fill is an image behind its children, and the root's is the bottom object. Corner radius or an elliptical crop on a picture is named, as a picture has no mask here. 11 unit tests for pictures and fonts in the importer and 2 more through the shipped binary (a `.gpx` project, a picture filed over MCP, a font dropped in `Fonts`, the ingest, and the refusals). **Pictures are now looked up in `Assets/Images/<frame>/` and fonts in `Fonts/` (user layout, 22 Nov 2026):** a picture fill finds `<image>.png`, `.webp`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg` or `.svg` there, in that order, and an SVG is drawn as the SVG it is, so a Figma vector node can arrive as its SVG without a PNG detour; a font is found as `.ttf`, `.otf`, `.woff2` or `.woff` by family and style. |
| `18.3` | Scene boundaries: one Figma frame is one scene | BE | 18.2, 11.29, 11.47 | Each top-level frame the user selects becomes exactly one scene. Its resolution is the frame's width × height, its origin is the frame's top-left, and its background is the frame fill. `clipsContent` is honoured as an 11.47 nested clip composition: content outside a clipping frame is kept but clipped, and never widens the scene. Nothing is re-parented across scenes. Nested frames stay nested groups with their own bounds and clipping. Multi-frame import produces N independent scene sessions **Built in part, 22 Nov 2026:** `scenes.ingest_figma` makes one scene from one frame per call (a page list or several frames refuse and store nothing): the canvas is the frame's width and height, positions are relative to the frame's top-left, the frame's fill is the bottom object, nested frames stay nested groups with child ids in Figma's order, and nothing is re-parented. **Left:** `clipsContent` as an 11.47 nested clip composition (today it is named on the report as not mapped), per-frame bounds and clipping on nested frames, and one call that takes several frames and returns N independent sessions. |
| `18.4` | Node and property coverage matrix | BE | 18.3, 1.2, 1.3 | Every Figma node type (frame, group, section, rectangle, ellipse, polygon, star, line, vector, boolean operation, text, component, instance, image fill) and property (per-corner radius, strokes with align/dash/cap/join, solid, linear, radial, angular and diamond gradients, image fill modes and crop, drop and inner shadow, layer and background blur, blend modes, opacity, masks, auto-layout resolved to absolute positions, constraints recorded) has a row in a committed coverage table marked **mapped**, **substituted (named)** or **refused (named)**, and each row has a test. Import never stops on an unsupported node: it becomes a named finding and the walk continues **Built in part, 22 Nov 2026:** mapped today: frame, group, section, rectangle and rounded rectangle (per-corner radii take the largest, named), ellipse, text, instance and component as groups or, as leaves, their picture; solid, linear and radial paints; strokes with weight (alignment named); picture fills with fit, fill, crop and tile; opacity, visibility, rotation and blend mode; a frame's own paint as a rectangle behind its children. Substituted and named: vector, line, star, polygon and boolean operation (their bounding box, or their exported picture or SVG when one is filed), drop and inner shadow and blurs (layer styles, 11.60). **Left:** the committed coverage table with a test per row, angular and diamond gradients, dash, cap and join, masks, auto-layout and constraints, and a walk that carries on past an unsupported node as a named finding for every node type (it does for the ones above). |
| `18.5` | Text fidelity | BE | 18.4, 11.13, 3.6 | Per-character style runs, line height, letter spacing, paragraph spacing, alignment, auto-resize modes and text case survive import. Every Figma font is resolved through 11.13 ingest; a font that cannot be resolved blocks the import by name instead of falling back **Built in part, 22 Nov 2026:** text content, size, line height, letter spacing (px to thousandths of an em), horizontal and vertical alignment, case and underline or strikethrough survive import as a text object; every family and style is one font declared by name, ready when its file is in the project's `Fonts` folder (otf, ttf, woff, woff2, matched by family and style) and otherwise missing and named, never a system font. **Left:** per-character style runs, paragraph spacing and indent, auto-resize modes, and making an unresolved font block the import by name (today the scene is stored with the font declared missing, and preflight refuses it). |

A regression fails CI. The target is 100% of corpus frames passing with zero unnamed substitutions |

Animation channels, phases, bindings and control fields on unchanged nodes are preserved. A deleted node that still carried animation or bindings is reported by name, not dropped silently |
