### Module B12 — Scripting, logic and plug-ins (runtime)

*Backend.* The runtime side of extending GrapiX: a deterministic sandbox for per-scene JavaScript, conditional logic and widgets, After Effects .jsx scripts running through undoable commands, and effect plug-ins from a Plug-ins folder, sandboxed. Any download needs the user's approval; agent authoring still goes through MCP. The script panels are module F5.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `16.4` | Script sandbox on rquickjs: deterministic per-scene JS | BE | 2.9, 11.36, 11.58 | CPU and memory bounded, disposable. No wall-clock access (`Date.now`, `performance.now`, timers) and no network (`fetch`, sockets); time comes only from the engine frame number and rational rate, data only through bindings, and typed APIs limit which objects a script may touch (invariant 62). A script calling a banned API is refused by name and the console (11.58) shows it; a fixture script driven only by frame number gives identical output across two runs; a script touching an object outside its declared scope is refused |
| `11.55` | Logic: conditional triggers, scene transitions, per-scene JS modules | BE | 6.6, 11.53, 11.54, 16.3, 16.4 | Conditional triggers, transitions between scenes and per-scene JS modules, all running inside 16.4's deterministic sandbox. A fixture trigger fires on the expected frame across two runs; a transition between two scenes renders identically across two runs; any banned API inside a module is refused by name |
| `11.56` | Widgets: clock, countdown, counter, round timer | BE | 11.55, 16.4 | Time is derived from the engine frame count and rational rate, never from the wall clock (invariant 62). At 60000/1001 a countdown shows exactly the rationally computed value after N frames; a long-run test shows zero drift; pause and resume are commands |
| `16.14` | After Effects scripting: .jsx scripts run against GrapiX through undoable commands | BE | 16.4, 11.36 | Scripts written for After Effects' documented object model (`app`, `app.project`, `CompItem`, layers, `Property` with keys and eases, `app.beginUndoGroup`) run in the Editor: File, Scripts lists a Scripts folder and Run Script File; every edit a script makes is an `EditorCommand` (undoable, journaled, the same as the UI's action); writing files and network access are off unless the user allows them (as After Effects' own setting); `#include` and `#target` are understood; `.jsxbin` is refused by name (no public specification). EffectCraft's scripting host is the component source (ADR-0003, attributed). Scripts are authoring only and can never cue, take or clear Program. From the user's direction of 18 Nov 2026 ("effects and presets shall be the main thing"; "support AE scripts or other extensions and plugins as After Effects does"); proposed by Claude. |
| `16.16` | Effect plug-ins: sandboxed modules from a Plug-ins folder | BE | 3.22, 16.4 | A third-party effect loads from a `Plug-ins` folder as a sandboxed WebAssembly module with declared typed parameters, appears in Effects & Presets under its own category, renders on the GPU path or through a bounded CPU bridge that can never delay Program (3.24), and is refused by name when it breaks its budget or its declaration. After Effects' native `.aex` plug-ins cannot run (a different ABI) and are refused by name. The WebAssembly runtime is a download that needs the user's approval. From the user's direction of 18 Nov 2026 ("effects and presets shall be the main thing"; "support AE scripts or other extensions and plugins as After Effects does"); proposed by Claude. |
| `16.24` | Transition model | BE | 16.3, 12.13 | A whole-frame commit; out then in; overlap with an offset that may be negative; a cut when the old scene has no exit; a per-layer default with a per-cue override; a stated policy for an interrupt. Two runs render identically. |
| `16.25` | JavaScript plugin runtime on the sandbox | BE | 16.4, 11.149 | An installed plugin may be a JavaScript module exporting a layout function of data, parameters and frame that returns shapes, run under the rules of 16.4 (bounded CPU and memory, no clock, no network, no files, banned APIs refused by name). Two runs give identical shapes. A plugin that fails keeps its last good output and one line reaches Playout diagnostics. WebAssembly plugins stay with 16.16. |

### Module B13 — Parity and approval

*Backend.* What a designer approves is what goes to air, from the engine side: viewport latency, binary alpha frames, engine picking, adaptive resolution, a pixel gate that holds the viewport equal to Program, the approval pipeline and provenance in use.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `13.1` | Measure viewport interaction latency on the existing stream | BE | 5.4 | Handle-drag round trip recorded on target hardware |
| `13.2` | Binary alpha frames | BE | 13.1 | Alpha correct at the viewport edge |
| `13.3` | Picking and bounds metadata from the engine | BE | 13.2 | Hit-testing without a browser renderer |
| `13.4` | Adaptive resolution | BE | 13.2 | Degrades resolution before latency |
| `13.6` | Pixel gate: viewport equals Program | BE | 4.4 | Byte-identical across the reference set |
| `13.7` | Approval pipeline | BE | 8.14, 11.41, 11.42, 13.6 | Changeset → headless engine render (11.41) → lint and conformance gate (11.42) → named human approval → hash-pinned revision (11.38) loadable by Playout. Only the Admin role may approve. A changeset that fails lint cannot be approved; Playout loads only `approved` revisions; the approval record names the approver and the time and is written to the JSON Lines audit trail (2.10) |
| `13.8` | Provenance in use | BE | 11.39, 13.7 | Playout refuses an asset whose licence class is incompatible with the show profile; a per-show AI-usage report is generated; an optional AI-disclosure label element is available. A fixture show profile refuses a fixture asset marked non-commercial; the report lists every model-origin asset used in a show |

> **Milestone.** What a designer approves is what goes to air.

## Frontend modules

The two Tauri apps' interfaces, built against the backend modules above. The Editor follows After Effects conventions, with XPression's layout knowledge; Playout follows XPression and Viz conventions.

### Module F1 — Editor workspace: the After Effects and XPression feel

*Frontend.* The Tauri 2 shell on Windows and the layout: the GrapiX identity, the dockable workspace, one command registry behind every menu, shortcut and agent action, the save model, tabbed groups, resizable and pop-out panels, named workspaces, a compact SKWAD live-mode UI, and the viewport with its overlays, gizmos and guides. Layout rows follow the measurements in docs/evidence/11.71.

Ctrl+Shift+S is the only way to create a new version: the next free numbered copy (`<name>_v002`, `_v003`, …) is written beside the current one, the previous version is left byte-identical, and the session continues in the new version. Neither shortcut fires while a text field has focus; a failed save keeps the unsaved mark and names its reason. **Built so far (23 Nov 2026):** File > Save and Ctrl+S save in place (the stored copy, else the scene file, else a first save into the open project or to a file) and never make a version; File > Save as next version and Ctrl+Shift+S write the session, with its unsaved edits, as the next numbered stored scene (`strap` gives `strap_v002`, named `Strap v002`, unpublished) or the next numbered file, leave the version it came from byte for byte as it was, and continue in the new one (a stored scene starts a fresh history; a file keeps its own). A scene saved nowhere gets a first save instead. Neither key fires in a text field. Ctrl K opens it from anywhere, a text field included, and again closes it: the one table of chords gets a `fromFields` flag that only this chord has, and the menu has Edit > Search everything… (Ctrl K). An empty box lists the commands that can run now. Every one of the 306 catalogued effects is findable, ported or not (invariant 86): only what is shown is limited, six per group with a line counting the rest. Help is left out until the help system exists (11.88). 15 search tests (scoring, order, the empty box, all 306 effects each first when its own name is typed, with a catalogue fed in reverse order so only an exact-name match can win, reasons, no `basedOn`), 2 key tests, 7 mutants caught; Editor 1,288 tests, typecheck and boundaries clean; the palette and the ribbon checked in a browser with sample rows. | `11.43` | Project settings, explicit save and single-file `.gpx` packaging | UI | 11.18, 11.29, 11.30, 11.36, 11.37, 11.38 | Work is saved as a `.gpx` project first. Scene create, rename, duplicate and delete are 11.36 commands, undoable through 11.37; scene settings cover resolution (1080p first), rational frame rate, duration, alpha on/off and background. Unsaved work cannot be published (named refusal); a non-rational frame rate cannot be entered and the attempt is refused. **Explicit save is the only project writer** (decision Q3, 16 Oct 2026): 2.6's autosave ring writes crash-recovery snapshots to a separate recovery folder, never into the project, and they are offered at next launch; 2.6's kill -9 mid-write test still passes **Project layout decided by the user, 22 Nov 2026: like XPression.** Every project is a named `.gpx` that holds the project, beside its supporting folders: `<Project>/<Project>.gpx`, `Images/<frame or folder>/`, `Footage/`, `Fonts/`. (XPression: the `.xpf` plus its Images and Fonts folders.) Assets are plain **PNG, or WebP as an option**, kept as files you can see and swap on disk, indexed in the `.gpx` with their hash, size and 11.39 provenance; vectors and SVG arrive as PNG at the exact design size, one folder per Figma frame. A single-file archive is produced only to hand over or publish (`.gpxpkg`, 1.8). Today's store is `<project>/scenes/*.json` with hash-named `assets/`, so this is a migration, not an addition: the legacy layout must read and upgrade losslessly. Stages: (1) the project document `<Project>.gpx` and its folders, replacing the settings file, with migration; (2) the asset registry: PNG and WebP checked by content and size, filed under `Images/<folder>/`, listed and imported through the Editor MCP; (3) Figma ingest resolves picture references against `Images/<frame>/` (the agent downloads the pictures into the project folder, GrapiX makes no network call) and builds image objects; (4) fonts in `Fonts/` turn a missing font ready by family and style; (5) footage and the save, open and recent-projects UI. **Built in part, 22 Nov 2026 (stages 1, 2 and 4 of the layout above):** a project is `<Project>/<Project>.gpx` (a versioned JSON document: schema version, name, settings; unknown fields and a name that does not match the folder refuse) beside `scenes/`, `Images/`, `Footage/` and `Fonts/`. A project made before it upgrades on its first write, keeping the colour space it declared (one with no settings file keeps the linear light it was made in, now written down); the document is written before the old settings file is removed, a document always wins over a stale old file, and a read changes nothing. Pictures are PNG or WebP files in `Images/<folder>/`: the bytes decide the format (a header check of PNG, and WebP lossy, lossless and extended), a file over 64 MiB or a side of 0 or over 16384 is refused, a name is one folder and one file name so nothing leaves `Images`, the same bytes filed again are a no-op and different bytes under a name refuse. The folder is the truth, so a file swapped on disk is the new picture: a picture is found by content through a hash cache keyed on length and modified time, so publish and the viewport read it with `read_asset` and it shows in `project_assets`. Fonts are TTF or OTF files in `Fonts/` (sfnt header checked), found by family and style however the file is named. MCP tool `assets.import_image` (`EditorCommand::ImportImage`, index 15) files a picture from an absolute path. Open: footage (stage 5), WebP and WOFF2 fonts, SVG to PNG conversion for vectors, provenance kept per picture in the `.gpx` (today in the scene's record), the save, open and recent-projects UI, and `.gpx` is still also the extension of a single-scene file, which should move so the two cannot be confused. **Layout revised by the user, 22 Nov 2026, and built: follow how XPression, Viz and After Effects projects work.** A project is created and saved at a path you choose first, and only then can work start (scene writes already refuse until the project exists; the New Project dialog that asks for a name and a folder, and Open Project from a `.gpx`, are the UI still to build; `Projects::open_gpx` is the store side of Open). `Footage/` is gone. The folders are `Assets/Images` (png, webp, jpg, jpeg, svg), `Assets/Videos` (mp4, mov, avi, webm), `Assets/Audio` (wav, mp3), `Assets/Models` (gltf, glb, obj, fbx, stl, ply, dae, usd, usdz) and `Fonts` (otf, ttf, woff, woff2), each one folder deep (a Figma frame's name, say), beside `scenes/` and the `<Project>.gpx`. The kind of a file is decided by its bytes (magic numbers, and for the text formats a check that it looks like one under its extension), a name must match what the bytes are, a file is filed once (the same bytes again are a no-op, different bytes under a name refuse), copies are streamed and hashed so a large video is never held in memory and is renamed into place, and a file is found by content wherever it lives, so a swapped file on disk is the new file. A header check, not a decoder: whether the engine can draw a codec or a mesh is answered at publish. What a scene retains (import sources, adopted assets) moved from `assets/` to `Sources/`, because on Windows `assets` and `Assets` are one folder; an earlier project's `assets`, `Images` and `Footage` are moved in place by the first write and a second upgrade changes nothing. `AssetKind` gained `audio`. The tool is `assets.import` (`EditorCommand::ImportAsset`, index 15; it replaces the earlier `assets.import_image`), which files one file by its kind. Open: an engine and Editor decision on which model formats and codecs actually render (glTF and GLB are the engine's first; the rest are stored and listed), audio and video in scenes, the New Project and Open Project dialogs and the recent-projects list, and renaming the single-scene `.gpx` file extension. **Start screen built, 22 Nov 2026 (user: "start building them"): the New Project and Open Project dialogs and the recent list.** The Editor now opens with no project and shows a modal Start dialog that cannot be dismissed (Escape is held back, there is no Cancel) until a project is open. New project asks for a name and a folder through the native folder picker and shows the exact path it will save to (`<folder>/<name>/<name>.gpx`); Open project picks a `.gpx`; the recent list shows each project newest first with how long ago, flags one whose `.gpx` has moved (not openable, can be forgotten, never silently dropped), and offers the projects still in the old default folder so earlier work is not stranded. File gained Open project and Close project, and choosing another project beside the open one opens it. The shell's refusal is shown as written with its correlation id (a name like CON, a folder that does not exist, a project already there). Behind it: `Projects::create_in` (the folder must exist and nothing is overwritten), `Projects::open_folder` (upgrades an older folder on the way in), `Workspace::switch_project` and `require_no_unsaved` (a scene with unsaved changes refuses the switch by name before anything is created or opened, so no work is lost and no stray project is left), `gx_editor::recent` (12 projects, disposable, atomic), and in the shell the open-project cell and eight commands (`cmd_new_project`, `cmd_open_project`, `cmd_open_project_folder`, `cmd_close_project`, `cmd_current_project`, `cmd_recent_projects`, `cmd_forget_recent_project`, `cmd_default_location_projects`). With no project open the shell's reads and writes refuse `projectNotFound` instead of falling back to a default folder. A switch that closes scenes reloads the page, which starts clean. Tests: 8 store, 5 shell logic (including the unsaved-changes block, asserted unconditionally), 9 web rules, and the dialog driven in a browser against a stubbed shell (list, new project form, folder picker, refusal, no dismissal on first run); not yet run in the real desktop window. **Run in the real Editor window, and Save As built, 23 Nov 2026 (user: "Check it in real desktop window. Also build save as").** The real window (the `gx-editor` shell and its WebView, driven through DevTools, no operating-system input; the workstation was locked, so a human has not looked at it) ran the Start screen, New project at 32 bits (the Scene Manager line reads `32 bpc float · sRGB`), a refused Save As and a whole Save As; the folder picker was answered at the page's IPC fetch, not seen. It found a defect: a new scene was always born at the 16 bit default, so **Save to project was refused (`sceneColorDepthMismatch`) in every project that was not 16 bit**. Fixed: the workspace remembers what the open project declares and a blank scene (and an imported SVG's depth) is born in it. **Save As** (File > Save project as…, and a Save as button): `Projects::save_copy` copies the whole project folder (Assets, Fonts, Sources, take pages, presets, design system, anything else; never the scene lock, a link or special file refuses by name) into a staging folder beside the destination, rewrites the `.gpx` under the new name with the same settings, writes into the copy the unsaved work of every scene stored in this project (each held to the revision check a save is, so another writer's change is never overwritten), and moves the folder into place as the last step; a failure removes the staging folder it made. The original is left exactly as it was last saved. `Workspace::save_project_as` refuses before copying anything: a scene with unsaved work that is not stored in this project (it would be lost by the switch), a stored scene that moved on, a name already in use (never overwritten), a bad name, a folder that does not exist or is inside the project being copied. It then switches to the copy and closes the open scenes as a project switch does; the page reloads onto the copy. Command `cmd_save_project_as`; the dialog is the New project form (name, folder, no depth field: a copy keeps the original's depth and space, which it says). Tests: 4 shell (the whole copy with unsaved work and the original byte for byte unchanged, every refusal leaving nothing behind, work after a copy into another folder lands in it, a new scene is born in the project's depth) and 1 web; 13 mutants of the copy and 5 of the new-scene fix killed (12 and 5 by an assertion; one, copying into the project itself, by a stack overflow from the endless self-copy it prevents). Evidence: `docs/evidence/11.43/`. **Found by the same gate:** the engine stopped on the operator's existing recovery journal (a scene hash no longer matched its pin after scene schema 4). Decided with Jev (E, 0.69) and Laya (C, uncalibrated), by hand: a journal whose scenes were written under another scene schema is refused by name before the hash is compared, saying what to do, and `gx-render-worker --discard-recovery` moves the journal, its asset store and drop report aside to dated `.bak` files and starts empty (`docs/evidence/11.43/jev-laya-journal.json`; 2 tests, 7 mutants killed; run on a copy of the real journal). The operator's own journal is untouched and still needs that flag. Open: a human look at the window and the native folder picker, copying off the window's thread with progress for a large project, keeping scenes open across a copy, and only the scene lock is held during the copy. |

| `11.104` | Pen tool: draw paths and make them shapes | UI | 11.102, 11.36, 3.13 | The Pen tool of the ribbon (key G) draws a path in the viewport: a click is a corner point, a click and drag pulls the point's two handles, Shift keeps the next point and a pulled handle to a multiple of 15 degrees (After Effects' step; a policy parameter, 45 on request), Alt while pulling breaks the handles apart, Space while placing moves the new point with its handles, a click on the first point closes the path (three or more points; a drag from it also sets the closing tangents) and makes a filled shape, Enter or a double click finishes an open path (two or more) as a white stroked line, Backspace takes a point back, Escape in the middle of placing a point takes that point back and otherwise ends the path and keeps what is placed (the spec's rule: Escape never throws a valid path away), a click on an end of the selected open shape carries that path on, and choosing another tool finishes a path of enough points. The path, its points and its handles are drawn while it is drawn, with the segment the pointer would add. Making the shape is ONE 11.36 batch (`createShape`, plus the paints that make an open path a line), so it is one undo step and the new shape is selected; a path the engine refuses is given back, not lost. A path never finishes in another scene than the one it was drawn in. | `11.112` | Animated paths: stable vertex ids and topology-preserving edits | BE | 11.107, 3.13 | A path's vertices can be keyframed; a vertex has a stable id across keyframes; an insert is applied at the same place to every keyframe so the shape at each is unchanged; a delete follows a stated policy; Set First Vertex is explicit; an incompatible topology holds rather than interpolates by index. A contract change with its generated TypeScript, refusals by name and golden frames. Every vertex and subpath also carries a stable id (the v2 spec's `anchorId` and `contourId`) that survives edits, undo and save; a path that a shape and a mask use is one shared path with a revision, a copy is independent unless the author links it (copy on write), and an edit names the revision it was made on so a stale one is refused. `BezierPath` is adapted by an additive contract change, not replaced and not duplicated. The spec's section 4 and 13, and the Pen v2 shared contract. (Built 24 Nov 2026, row stays active. **Done:** `BezierPath.vertexIds`, `SceneTimeline.pathChannels` (a key is a whole path) and `SceneDocument.pathLinks`, all optional and absent from an old scene's JSON, with generated TypeScript. Keys blend by vertex id with the easing of the key they leave; keys that differ in open/closed, count or ids hold the earlier key and say why by name; before the first and after the last key the path holds. An insert goes into every key with one new id and splits each curve exactly (De Casteljau), a delete takes the vertex out of every key, reverse and Set First Vertex turn ids with the vertices, a key at a frame is set by `editPath` with a frame, `setPathKey`, `removePathKey` and `movePathKey`. The CPU reference re-prepares the path each frame; the GPU compositor sizes the layer source to the union of every key's drawn bounds (grown by how far a control polygon passes its curve) and draws it again into the same texture each frame, mask weights of an effect's Mask Reference included: DX12 and Vulkan, sRGB and linear, within one code value of the reference, frames scrubbed in any order. A path shared by a shape and a mask (or two masks) is a `PathLink` with a revision: a copy stays independent, `linkPaths` makes two paths one, an `editPath` writes every user and may name its `baseRevision` (a stale one is refused by name), `unlinkPath` ends it, a mask is held by id so reordering keeps the link, and delete and mask removal end it. Editor: the path at the playhead, Key path, Key path edits, Remove path key, path keys as timeline tracks (move, delete, ease), Link to shape and Unlink in the Masks panel. Decisions: Jev A at 0.91 (representation) and A at 1.0 (shared path); Laya's C and B overridden, they contradict the specs (`docs/evidence/11.112/`). Also (24 Nov 2026): a stable id per subpath (`ShapeObject.contourIds`, absent until needed, kept by `setShapePath` for the subpaths that stay, Jev A at 0.82, Laya C overridden) and `removeSubpath`, which takes a subpath out with its channels and links ending and the later ones following to their new places; a linked path animates as one (every user holds the same keys, a key set, moved or removed on any user is on all, the revision goes up, the validator holds the keys equal, linking onto a path that has keys of its own is refused by name). **Not done:** the Editor links a mask to a shape (the engine links any two paths); the per-frame redraw costs about 13 ms a frame for two animated sources (a filled and stroked shape and a masked rect with an effect reference) on an RTX 3070 Ti in a DEBUG test build, a settled frame at 26 to 28 ms against 13 ms with no path animation, with a blocking readback in both; a release build and a long scene are not measured, and the redraw allocates its drawing buffers every frame, which a release build would want pooled. 24 Nov 2026 (found with jevgrep): the redraw no longer copies the whole evaluated document for each animated layer on each frame; it copies the one object, swaps it in and puts the object list back through a drop guard (also on a panic). Debug build, 2,000 shapes with 200 eight-key path channels: 6.8 ms per frame per animated layer before, 1.3 microseconds after; the animated path GPU test still matches the reference on DX12 and Vulkan. Jev and Laya both call it behaviour-preserving (`docs/evidence/11.112/jev-laya-d5-frame-copy.json`).) |

*Frontend.* What the author works in: Scene Manager, object manager, inspector, fonts, materials, assets, data bindings, text styles, the Test fill and the console. Every panel is a projection of the command model and reachable from the Window menu.

### Module F3 — Animation: timeline, keyframes and easing

*Frontend.* After Effects behaviour: per-property tracks, keyframe diamonds and Auto-Key, selection-driven timeline editing, easing and Bezier curves, the speed graph, stagger and infinity modes. Every property is animatable, and every imported Figma or PSD layer is an ordinary animatable object.

**Editor 2.0 parity and animation requirements.** Tracked by 11.18 to 11.24 and 11.68; they do not claim that the existing component-level steps deliver a complete editor.

- Visual direction: keep the UI clean, minimal and Figma-like; target roughly 70% Figma simplicity and 30% simplified After Effects animation control. Reveal advanced controls only when needed; do not copy After Effects complexity.
- Editor 1.x parity is required in Editor 2.0: restore the templates/scenes manager, object creation and object manager, object details and type-aware property inspector, data binding panel, font manager, asset browser and material manager as reachable, functional panels in the Tauri workspace.
- Inspect and reuse the existing architecture before implementation. Extend the current object-manager, inspector, scene-manager, font-manager, material-manager, motion-preset-browser, agent-surface and viewport modules and Rust editor services; do not unnecessarily rewrite working systems or create a second scene model.
- Place keyframe diamonds beside supported animatable properties, including Position, Scale, Rotation, Opacity and Size. Empty means no keyframe at the current playhead; filled means a keyframe exists there. Indicate animation elsewhere separately so an empty diamond does not imply the whole track is unanimated. Clicking adds or removes the current-frame key, with accessible labels and keyboard operation.
- Provide a clearly visible Auto-Key mode. When enabled, changing an animatable property at a new timeline position creates a keyframe; editing an existing key updates it without duplicates. Specify base-value and first-key behavior, multi-selection, undo/redo and Auto-Key-off behavior before implementation so edits do not unexpectedly alter other frames.
- Selecting an object automatically reveals its animation tracks. Expand/collapse tracks by property and keep selection synchronized across viewport, object manager, inspector and timeline. Support selecting, dragging/moving, deleting and copying/pasting keyframes, with frame snapping, defined collision behavior and one undo transaction per gesture.
- Offer Linear, Ease In, Ease Out, Ease In-Out and Custom Bezier easing. Keep common easing one action away and show the curve editor on demand. Add simple Fade, Slide, Scale and Pop presets through the existing preset library, with adjustable supported parameters and save-selection-as-preset.
- Spacebar toggles smooth local animation preview and pause from the current playhead; it must not steal typing or native control shortcuts when an input, editable field or button has focus. Scrubbing and playback evaluate the same animation at the scene rational frame rate, preserve editing state and never send Program/take/output commands.
- Preserve Rust-owned contracts and generated TypeScript, validated writes, revision checks, undo transactions, design-system motion tokens and the existing preview/production separation. Reconcile editable scene channels with existing normalized preset channels and easing tokens; extend contracts only where necessary. Human keyframe authoring must not silently widen the agent/MCP surface, which selects and parameterizes presets under ADR Part G.
- Acceptance requires actual Tauri workflows on Windows: create/open a scene or template, add/select/edit objects, bind data, manage fonts/assets/materials, animate with diamonds and Auto-Key, edit/copy keys, apply easing/presets, preview, undo/redo, save and reopen. Verify animation persistence and evaluated values, report unsupported cases, and retain the 11.10 flat-node-count and 11.11 no-static-repaint gates. Isolated panel projections or headless tests alone do not establish Editor 1.x parity.

| `11.68` | Keyframes the way After Effects and EffectCraft do them: per-property tracks, easing, graph editor | UI | 11.36 | Every animatable property has its own keyframe track with stopwatch-style enable, keyframe selection and nudging, easy ease and custom bezier easing, and a value and speed graph editor, matching the After Effects interaction model and the EffectCraft engine semantics the render core ports; the engine renders the same curve the Editor shows (a parity test at keyframes and between them). From the user's product direction of 18 Nov 2026 and the market scan `docs/research/market-scan-2026-11-18.md`; proposed by Claude. **Audited 18 Nov 2026.** What exists (11.10, 11.11, 11.36): per-property channels, a stopwatch-style toggle and key nudging (`keyframe-editor.ts`, `timeline/key-edit.ts`), named easings and a custom bezier stored per segment (`(x1,y1)` is the outgoing handle and `(x2,y2)` the incoming one, so the **contract already carries After Effects' in and out influence**), a TypeScript easing mirror pinned against the Rust evaluator (`timeline/easing.ts`), and a value-over-time graph module (`timeline/speed-graph.ts`, 12 tests). **Gaps found:** the graph draws only the outgoing handle and `bezierFromHandleDrag` is not wired to any pointer handling or command in the app, so no curve can be edited in it; no incoming-handle display or drag; no After Effects "Easy Ease" (33.33% influence, `(1/3,0,2/3,1)`) presets; no true speed (velocity) graph beside the value graph; no multi-key selection of handles; the engine-versus-Editor parity test between keys is the existing pinned reference only. No contract change is needed for the first build: incoming handle, Easy Ease presets, and wiring the drag to the existing edit command. The graph's canvas theme was still hard-coded blue and is now the SKWAD ember (done). Needs Jev and Laya on the interaction model before the build. **First build done, 18 Nov 2026; the row stays open** (`docs/evidence/11.68`). The interaction model is After Effects' own, so it was not put to Jev and Laya. Built: both handles of a segment are drawn and edited (the contract already stored them: `(x1,y1)` out, `(x2,y2)` in); dragging a handle on the value graph shows the curve live and saves one undoable `updateKeyframe` gesture on release; **Easy Ease** (speed 0, 33.33% influence) on the selected keys by F9 (both sides), Shift+F9 (in), Ctrl+Shift+F9 (out), or the Easing picker, rewriting the arriving segment on the previous key and the leaving segment on the key itself and keeping each segment's other handle; a read-only **speed graph** beside the value graph (a button in the timeline header); the graph is now a continuous curve (it was drawn as one flat step per frame) and uses the SKWAD ember. Easy Ease is exactly smoothstep `3t^2 - 2t^3`, pinned on the engine evaluator (`easy_ease_is_smoothstep_on_the_engine_evaluator`) and in the Editor (`Editor/tests/easy-ease.test.ts`, 17 tests) with the same numbers. Seen in the running Editor: handle drag, F9 and Shift+F9 giving handles at exactly one third and two thirds, the speed parabola, and the playhead no longer jumping after a drag (found live, fixed). 13 mutations each failed a named test. **Not built, and named:** dragging handles in the speed graph (After Effects edits influence there); multi-key handle editing; a custom bezier numeric editor beyond the key detail panel; temporal handles on separate dimensions (X and Y are separate channels here by design); the pointer glue in `main.ts` (drag, the click after it) has no unit test and is covered by the live check only; the timeline dock is short on a default layout, so the graph is hard to use until it is enlarged (a layout item for 11.67). |
| `11.77` | Stagger animations: offset the keys of several selected objects by a step | UI | 11.22 | With several objects selected, a Stagger command offsets each one's selected keys by a step (frames, in the order of selection or the layer order, with a reverse and a from-centre option), as one undoable gesture, ported from XPression's Stagger Animations. From the user's direction of 18 Nov 2026 ("borrow the XPression UI knowledge from the xpression-builder skill and work out the modules that can be incorporated and improvise the UI layouts by optimally using the real estate on the application"); design read by Jev (Laya answered 0.90 to 0.95 to every yes/no question and disagreed on every choice, so Jev led and this is disclosed); proposed by Claude. Evidence: `docs/evidence/11.71`. Jev 0.77. **Built 26 Nov 2026 (the KEYFRAME bar's Stagger button and the Keys group's settings; Editor only, because the result is the same batch of `moveKeyframe` / `movePathKey` edits a drag sends, so there is no new command and an agent sends the same batch; Jev A on all five questions, Laya A on four and C on one at confidence 0.022 (the step in seconds; kept frames, row 11.135), `docs/evidence/11.77/jev-laya-stagger.json`):** with keys selected on at least two elements, Stagger moves only the SELECTED keys, grouped by element: the elements are put in layer order (the timeline's layer rows, top first) or selection order (the order the first key of each element was selected), and the element at position i moves its keys by i steps, so the first one stays; Reverse makes the first one the last to move; From centre ranks each element by its distance from the middle (the middle one, or the middle pair, stays; needs three elements); the step is whole frames, default 5, negative to stagger earlier, and 0 or a fraction is refused by name; any key that cannot land (a frame another key holds, or outside the timeline) refuses the WHOLE gesture; one undo takes it all back, and the selection follows the keys that moved. `Editor/src/timeline/stagger.ts` (the pure planner), `planShift` in `key-edit.ts` (`planMove` is now a wrapper of it, unchanged in behaviour: 59 timeline tests pass as before), `keyframe-bar.ts` (the button and its dimmed reasons), `Editor/web/{main.ts,index.html,style.css}` (Step, Order, Reverse and From centre in the Keys group). **Verified:** 33 Editor tests (`stagger.test.ts`) and the bar test updated; 23 of 23 mutants caught (`docs/evidence/11.77/mutation-log.txt`); Editor suite 109 files, 1,719 passed, 1 skipped; typecheck, `codegen:check` and `check:boundaries` clean; seen in a real Editor window: two rects keyed at frame 0 and 20, Stagger +5 left rect-1's key at 20 and moved rect-2's to 25 with both still selected and the history line `Stagger 2 elements by +5 frames`, one Undo put both back, step 20 on the frame-0 keys refused with `frame 20 on rect-2.opacity already has a key` and nothing moved, step 0 and From centre with two elements refused by name. Not seen live: From centre and Reverse with three elements (unit-tested). **Not built:** Stagger of colour keys (codex's 11.132 colour channels are not yet in the timeline), a Stagger of selected whole layers, and the order of selection taken from the order the objects were selected (it is the order the keys were). |

| `11.134` | Graph editor: speed graph, ease presets and numeric ease | UI | 11.68, 11.11 | The Graph shows value and speed graphs with handles, ease presets (Linear, Ease, Ease in, Ease out, Back, Bounce, Elastic, Hold), influence and speed numbers, and apply, copy and paste ease. Ease out sets x1 of the curve leaving the selected key; Ease in sets x2 of the PREVIOUS key's curve as one minus the typed percentage; both write a custom bezier on the key that owns the segment, keep the handle's height (a Linear segment stays straight: its handle slides along the line), skip a Hold segment (a dash is shown where there is no curve), hold a typed number to 0.1 to 100 percent, and edit every selected channel of the moment together (one undoable batch). `planSetInfluence` (`key-card.ts`), the field (`key-card-view.ts`), the handler (`web/main.ts`); 12 tests and 11 mutants caught; 1,394 Editor tests and the strict typecheck pass; not seen in the running Editor. Jev (A, A, A, 0.98, 0.79, 0.96) was followed; Laya agreed on two and chose conversion to a bezier on Hold with a confidence of 0.02 (`docs/evidence/figma-ui/jev-laya-influence.json`). **Back presets built 25 Nov 2026:** the card's Over time list offers Back out (0.34, 1.56, 0.64, 1) and Back in (0.36, 0, 0.66, -0.56) beside the named easings; each writes that single cubic bezier on every selected key with a segment leaving it, and a key whose curve is one of them reads as it. A property with a range (opacity 0 to 100 percent, width and height not negative) refuses a Back whose overshoot would leave the range, naming the property and the frames, checked along the whole curve. `planSetPreset`, `BEZIER_PRESETS` (`key-card.ts`); 5 tests and 12 mutants caught; 1,408 Editor tests pass; not seen in the running Editor. Jev A, A (1.0, 1.0), Laya A and B (0.18, 0.23), `docs/evidence/figma-ui/jev-laya-presets.json`. **Bounce and Elastic built 25 Nov 2026** (the owner: yes, build bounce and elastic). Two new named easings in the contract, `bounce` and `elastic`, each the out form and each fixed forever as Robert Penner's easeOutBounce and easeOutElastic (amplitude 1, period 0.3). `ease_at` solves them by their own formulas (the engine, the Rust channel and path-morph evaluators all go through it), the TypeScript mirror (`easing.ts`) is pinned by the generated reference table, which now carries both (they have no control points, so they are left out of `NAMED_EASING_POINTS`), and design-system easing tokens `bounce` and `elastic` map to them. Bounce never leaves the range between its two keys. Elastic swings past the second key by about 37 percent of the distance, so a property with a range refuses it where that would leave the range: the contract checks the whole curve at 4,096 samples when keys are set or loaded (`KeyframeValueOutOfDomain`, naming the key), and the Editor says it first, naming the property and the frames (opacity 0 to 1 is refused, 0.5 to 0.6 is fine; a size falling to 0 is refused). The card's Over time list offers both; a segment that is Hold, Bounce or Elastic shows no Ease in or Ease out number and no graph handles (a handle would suggest a curve that is not there, and dragging it would silently replace the easing), and the graph samples them finely so its range shows the first peak. 5 contract tests (Penner's values, bounds and overshoot, wire names and tokens, the range refusals, channel evaluation), 8 Editor tests, `codegen` regenerated; mutations: 15 Rust (12 caught, 3 survived: one real, the last bounce arc, killed by a new sample; two equivalent: removing the exact-1 early return still gives exactly 1.0, and equal keys cannot overshoot) and 14 TypeScript (12 caught, 1 survived and was killed by a sharper range test, 1 equivalent). Jev A, A, A (1.0), Laya A, C, A (0.13, 0.38, 0.07; Laya would allow Elastic and let the renderer clamp), `docs/evidence/figma-ui/jev-laya-bounce.json`. **A scene saved with either easing cannot be read by an older Editor or engine** (the closed list grew), so both sides must be updated together. **Not built:** in and in-out forms of either (the vocabulary stays small on purpose), a speed number beside the influence, copy and paste of an ease, and dragging handles in the speed graph. |

a wrong one is named), UTF-8 only with one byte order mark removed (UTF-16 and a code page refused by name), blank lines only at the end, empty or repeated column names, ragged rows, stray and unterminated quotes refused, stated limits (16 MiB, 100,000 rows, 256 columns, 64 KiB a cell), cells exactly as written. 26 tests, 55 of 55 mutants caught; `column_change` names the columns added, removed or reordered between two files, a rename never guessed. 45 tests, 28 of 28 mutants caught. Decided for the next slice: a source is a copy in the project (a `Data/` folder in the `.gpx`, filed like an asset), read from the copy; an update is explicit, shows the change and keeps the old copy until accepted; a record is its 1-based row number; an outside path enters only by the import copy, from the Editor's file picker. Still to build: keeping a source in the project (codex's `project.rs`), a published sheet, the shell command, a refusal for the conformance audit, codegen, and the panel's Test records. **Designed, 26 Nov 2026 (`docs/design/data-block-2026-11-26.md`, Jev A on 3 of 3, `docs/evidence/11.153/jev-laya-data-block.json`):** Hide when empty is an optional boolean on the object (refused on an unbound element; the engine does not draw an element whose bound value is missing, null or blank); the record check is the engine's, one request over the scene and a list of records using the renderer's shaping, answering Fits, Overflows (px, lines), Empty or Missing per record and bound text; overflow means the shaped text is larger than its box where the layout does not grow. Split into codex slices A (hideWhenEmpty), B1 (the fit report), B2 (the request and shell command), C (records: sample data as one, then the CSV kept in the project) and the Editor slice D, which starts when A and B2 are released. Until then the Editor draws no FITS nobody measured. **Built, 26 Nov 2026 (claude, codex's limit being used up; `docs/log/2026-11-26.md`):** slice A end to end (hideWhenEmpty: contract, resolver and its Editor twin through three new cases of the shared binding fixture, engine witness, Editor Data section and card buttons); B1 (`Shared/contracts/src/fit_report.rs`, `services/render-engine/worker/src/fit.rs`: the fit report measured with the renderer's own shaping); B2 (the asset plane's `fit` request, the engine's `prepare_fit`, `gx_editor::fit`, the shell's `cmd_fit_report`); C in part (the scene's sample data is the one record `sample`); D in part (the Data screen's Test records block: Check records, the tally, Checks, a row per record; a line per problem on the element's card). **Not built:** records from a CSV kept in the project (slice C, task 121 slice 3), the Publish screen's Test row, Auto-fit drawn by the engine (row 11.51). **The Publish screen's Test row built, 27 Nov 2026 (claude, `Editor/src/publish-checks.ts`, Jev and Laya `docs/evidence/11.153/jev-laya-publish-test-row.json`):** drawn after Data when a text is bound to data; a Test that says FIX blocks Publish like the other rows (Jev B at 0.75, Laya B), before a check or when the graphic changed since it says so in words and never blocks and never reads OK, the check runs when the designer asks (a Check records button on the row, the Data screen's report shared), and the engine's problem is shown in words; seen in a real window: not checked, then the unreachable engine's reason; mutants 15 of 15. Not yet seen with a real engine on the Publish row (the report itself was, on the Data screen). **CSV in the Data screen (27 Nov, Editor side of slice C):** the CSV file button is live (Replace CSV file once one is there); a delimiter box (comma, semicolon or tab, in words or as the character, anything else refused in words) is stated before the picker opens; the import calls `cmd_import_project_csv`, the source is listed first and selected with its file, records and columns, and a graphic reopened from its project asks `cmd_project_csv_source` once; a successful import drops the old report (gone, not stale), so nothing says FITS that was not measured against this file; 12 new data-panel tests, mutants 12 of 12. **Seen in a real window (27 Nov):** a 48-record CSV imported through the native picker, listed as the source, and the check read 46 FIT and 2 NEED FIXING with record 17 OVERFLOWS 685.5 PX and record 33 EMPTY, each named in Checks, and the Publish Test row said FIX with the same tally (`docs/evidence/11.153/live-csv-27-nov.txt`); the live look found the delimiter box was not drawn (an input needs an edit action), fixed with a test. Not yet seen: replacing a source (the prompt), a cancelled picker, a reopened project restoring its source, a screenshot of the screens after import. **Not built:** Auto-fit drawn by the engine (row 11.51). Done when a CSV of 48 records, one overflowing and one empty, shows 46 FIT and 2 to fix, each named, in the panel and as the Publish screen's Test row. |
| `11.154` | Installed fonts, Arial as the default font, WOFF and WOFF2 conversion | UI | 11.13 | The font manager lists the fonts installed on this PC and takes the picked ones into a scene stored as WOFF2, WOFF or as installed; Arial is the default font, so adding text to a scene with no font works at once; every conversion is decoded again and compared table by table before it is stored. **Built, 28 Nov 2026** (`Shared/asset-plane/src/fonts/convert.rs`, `system.rs`; shell `cmd_system_fonts_list`, `cmd_font_prepare_system`, `cmd_font_prepare_default`; `Editor/src/font-manager.ts`, the Installed fonts section of the Fonts panel): the list reads the machine's and the user's Windows font folders, a few tables per file, a collection (.ttc) face by face, and names every file it could not read; Add installed fonts opens the list (search, pick several, Store as WOFF2 / WOFF / Keep as installed) and Add Arial takes in Arial regular, bold, italic and bold italic from the PC's own copy as WOFF2; adding text to a scene with no font takes Arial in first (its own undo entry). **Decisions:** Arial is **not downloaded from a website**: it is a Monotype font with no free source, and a copy from a mirror is an unlicensed copy from an untrusted host. It is taken from the PC's own Windows copy, which the user is licensed for; a PC without Arial regular is refused by name and nothing is put in its place. Installed fonts are always `Restricted` (they stay on this PC and never enter a package); packaging a font goes through Add font files with a licence the author states. WOFF2 is written at Brotli quality 9 (about 3 s for the four Arial faces against about 40 s at quality 11, files 3 to 5 per cent larger). **Not built:** the WOFF2 glyf/loca transform (tables are Brotli compressed but stored untransformed, which every decoder reads; the reference encoder's files are smaller still); no re-conversion of a font already in a scene; variable-font axes are not read; the Draft view still draws no text. Evidence: `docs/evidence/11.154/`. |

### Module F7 — Preview and parity

*Frontend.* The Editor's engine preview monitor, and the browser reduced to overlays only, so what a designer approves is what goes to air. Fed by module B13.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `11.65` | Engine preview monitor in the Editor | UI | 11.41 | The Preview panel shows an engine-rendered frame (11.41) of the active graphic at the playhead, labelled "Engine", within 250 ms of an edit on the reference machine (method recorded); the Three.js viewport is labelled "Draft"; a disconnected or refusing engine shows a named unavailable state, never a stale frame presented as current; parity with Program is not claimed until 13.6. Verified in the running Tauri Editor. Scheduled ahead of 11.31–11.35 (decision Q2) **First build done, 18 Nov 2026; the row stays open** (`docs/evidence/11.65`). Design reviewed by Jev and Laya (text only): asset-plane preview (Jev 0.96, Laya agreed), latest-only coalescing (0.76, agreed), a labelled out-of-date frame (0.98; Laya preferred blank, not taken). Built: a closed `preview` request on the asset plane (`Shared/asset-plane/src/publish.rs`: the draft `SceneDocument` and a frame; reply is the PNG with its SHA-256, re-hashed by the client; named refusals; the peer builds a job under its lock and the **render runs outside it**, invariant 14); the engine prepares the draft transiently (cached by content hash, 4 entries), draws it on the CPU reference (which draws effects, masks and clips) behind the same detached gate as headless renders so a preview never competes with Program under load (3.24), creates no revision and touches nothing on air; `gx_editor::preview::preview_document` and the shell's `cmd_engine_preview` (async, takes a target never a document, no control-plane client: R6 holds); `Editor/src/engine-preview.ts`, a pure scheduler: one render in flight and one pending, the newest wins, a frame is `current` only when it is the latest request's, otherwise `Out of date`, and an unreachable or declining engine is `Engine unavailable` or `Engine declined` with the reason; the Preview panel shows the Engine frame with its status and render time and the viewport is labelled Draft. Seen in the running Editor: a rectangle drawn by the engine, then an orange soft drop shadow beside it that the Draft viewport cannot draw. Tests: 7 asset-plane dialect, 4 engine, 2 editor, 13 scheduler; 13 mutations each failed a named test; the CSP now allows `blob:` images. **Open, and named:** the render takes about 0.6 to 1.0 s on this debug engine against the row's 250 ms, so the budget is **not met or measured**: it needs a release build, the reference machine and a recorded method; a draft that uses assets the engine does not hold is refused by name (`AssetMissing`) because the preview does not yet send them; no preview while the Draft plays; no GPU path (the GPU compositor serves Program); no zoom or compare. **Viewport takes the engine frame, 19 Nov 2026** (user: "wire the effects to the viewport"): the main viewport now shows the engine's frame when an enabled effect is present, because the Draft view cannot draw effects and left those objects out; a View switch (Auto, Draft, Engine) in the composition header, the chip and a note say which picture it is, and while playing or when the engine has no frame it falls back to Draft and says so (`Editor/src/viewport-source.ts`, 10 tests). The selection overlay stays on top of either picture. Typecheck and 541 Editor tests pass; not yet seen in the running Editor, and the budgets above are unchanged. |

## Later — parked, kept with their IDs and dependencies

Parked on 18 November 2026 because they are not part of the EffectCraft-core application as it is now being built. Nothing here was deleted; each module can be pulled back into the main order when the user says so.

### Module X1 — Later: remote engine

*Backend.* A deployment change, not an architecture change, if the earlier modules held the line: QUIC and mTLS, the control plane over a remote transport, resumable assets, codec negotiation, NVENC, two engine instances by role, the build hash pinned at publish, and the conformance suite at L1. Parked 18 Nov 2026 with its IDs and dependencies.

| `14.4` | Media codec negotiation by locality and consumer role | BE | 5.5 | Interactive and observational streams differ |
| `14.5` | NVENC encode | BE | 14.4 | Bandwidth within a LAN budget |

| `14.8` | Conformance suite passes at L1 exactly as at L0 | BE | 5.6 | Same suite, remote transport |

### Module X4 — Later: GrapiX Data Server (services)

*Backend.* A DataLinq-class standalone application that holds many data sources and serves both the Editor and Playout, bounded by an ADR before any code (19.1). Two constraints carry in from existing invariants and may not be relaxed by that ADR: the engine never fetches data itself and a take stays intent-based, and the data a frame was rendered with is recorded with the take, so a frame can be reproduced. Parked 18 Nov 2026.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `19.1` | ADR-0005: Data Server boundaries | CON | 1.9, 5.1 | Accepted ADR fixing: process model (desktop app plus headless service mode), transport (reuses the 5.x control plane and gx1 tokens, 2.9), who resolves a binding at take time (Playout, never the engine), how values reach the engine (as data carried with the take intent), revision pinning of data, and failure policy per field (refuse, hold last good, or warn) |
| `19.2` | Data contract | CON | 19.1 | `DataSource`, `DataTable`, `DataRecord` and typed `DataField` values, plus a source revision and timestamp and a staleness state, defined in Rust (`gx-contracts`) with TS generated; round-trips both ways; a schema change is a named event, never silent |
| `19.4` | Connectors: files and web | BE | 19.2 | CSV, Excel (xlsx), JSON and XML from a file, HTTP (with a poll interval) or WebSocket, RSS/Atom, and manually edited tables. Each connector detects schema drift and names it, and a file watch uses 2.5 |
| `19.5` | Connectors: databases and sheets | BE | 19.4 | SQL through PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQL Server and ODBC; Google Sheets. Source credentials live in the OS keychain and never appear in scenes, packages, logs or the MCP surface |
| `19.6` | Many sources, change propagation and staleness | BE | 19.4 | Multiple sources run concurrently. Subscribers get a snapshot then ordered deltas. The last good value is retained, and staleness past a source's declared limit is surfaced to every subscriber (never silently stale). A deliberately stalled fixture feed produces its declared 19.1 state (hold last good or refuse the update), never a guessed value. Reconnect resumes from a revision without a full reload |
| `19.8` | Playout reads the Data Server | BE | 19.6, 6.2, 6.3 | At cue/take, Playout resolves each bound field from the server and sends the values with the intent. The committed frame records the data revision it used. A missing or stale field follows the 19.1 policy and names the field; a feed value whose type does not match the bound field is refused by name. Output is never blocked on a slow source without saying so |
| `19.9` | Data Server soak and failure tests | BE | 19.8 | Eight hours with at least 20 sources at mixed update rates, source outages, server restarts and network loss; zero silent staleness and zero takes rendered with unrecorded data |
| `19.10` | Live esports data and an AI suggestion lane | BE | 19.8, 11.55 | Esports feeds (GRID or others) ingest into a typed, timestamped model with deterministic triggers; data reaches the engine only as Control-plane intent. Suggestions need producer confirmation unless their class is pre-approved. Replaying a recorded feed produces the same trigger sequence on every run; a suggestion never changes Program without a confirm action or a pre-approved class |
| `19.11` | Per-source adapter sandbox | BE | 16.4, 19.3 | Each source can run a sandboxed JavaScript adapter under the rules of 16.4, with its own console, sample capture and a path picker. A banned API is refused by name. |
| `19.12` | Live transports: SSE, webhook, TCP, UDP, serial and MQTT | BE | 19.4 | Each transport delivers values with a source revision and sequence; a lost transport marks the source stale; Playout keeps the last good values and says so. |
| `19.13` | Recorded feed capture and deterministic replay (Replay Lab) | BE | 19.11 | A feed can be recorded and replayed to the engine on the same frames; replay reproduces the same values and the same as-run record. |
| `19.14` | Data Hub decision: fourth application or service | CON | 19.3 | Owner decision recorded: invariant 1 is either amended by the owner or Data Hub is restated as a service that never touches Program. Until then 19.3 stays parked. No invariant is edited by this row. |
| `19.15` | Opted-in live fields | BE | 11.54, 19.8, 12.11 | Fields marked live accept typed deltas with source revision and sequence, applied as frame-numbered intent, with a staleness policy per field (hold, refuse or warn) and an as-run record per update. The take guarantee is stated in three layers: package version, checked first data, permitted live updates. |

|---|---|---|---|---|
| `16.10` | Generative asset adapters | AI | 11.39, 11.52 | Pluggable generators for images, 3D and video loops, baked offline into hashed assets with a licence class; no GPL or non-commercial model is bundled, and region-restricted models are flagged. Every generated asset carries a complete 11.39 provenance record; an adapter whose model licence class is not set is refused; nothing generative runs at Take time (invariant 63) |
| `16.11` | AI keyer sidecar | BE | 15.6 | Matting runs as a separate supervised process publishing fill+key on the Media plane; the engine uses the latest key that arrives in time and drops late keys (invariant 14). Killing the keyer causes no Program frame drop; the layer goes to its declared "no key" state and the console raises an alarm |
| `16.12` | Gaussian splat layer | BE | 11.48, 13.6 | `KHR_gaussian_splatting` glTF loads with deterministic sorting. Two runs give identical golden frames; the frame-time budget holds at 1080p |
| `16.13` | Interchange: OpenUSD/MaterialX import, OpenTimelineIO export | BE | 11.50, 11.53 | OpenUSD/MaterialX import maps onto 11.50's unified material, and sequencer timelines export as OpenTimelineIO. **Done-when not yet defined:** it must be written before this step may start |
| `16.20` | Tracked camera and virtual studio | BE | 3.7, 16.8, 16.19 | Later, optional. FreeD over UDP (and serial if feasible) drives the scene's 3D camera: position, rotation and field of view from a lens table, a manual delay in frames, a monitor showing the rate and dropped packets, a switch to ignore or override each value, picture and key output, and a manual camera as the fallback. The FreeD scaling differs between sources and must be checked against real hardware. Other tracking systems, OpenTrackIO, lens distortion files and timecode sync follow. Research: docs/evidence/xpression-benchmark/research.md. |
| `16.21` | Mask tracking and auto-trace | BE | 3.19, 11.112 | Parked. A mask follows footage by position, rotation, scale, skew or perspective found from tracked points, with outliers dropped, a confidence reported and a simpler model chosen when the points are degenerate; the track is kept apart from the artist's own path edits so a retrack never overwrites them. A channel (alpha, red, green, blue or luma) is traced into Bezier masks within a stated pixel tolerance. Both run as bounded background jobs and are frozen into the scene, so the engine never waits for them. Masking spec v2, sections 9.2 to 9.4. |
| `16.22` | Roto brush with a local segmentation worker | AI | 16.11, 16.21 | Parked and optional. Foreground and background hints give a matte through a separate local worker (SAM 2 is a candidate; nothing is downloaded without approval and no cloud is needed). The result is frozen as a hashed matte sequence the engine plays with no model present, and the model name, version and hash are recorded. Masking spec v2, sections 9.5 and 9.6. |
| `16.23` | Matte refinement, keyers and mask motion blur | BE | 3.19, 3.20 | Parked. Choke, shift edge, edge contrast and a guided refine on a soft matte; garbage and holdout mattes; luma, chroma-distance and difference keys as matte sources; Set Matte and channel routing for old projects; and a mask sampled across the shutter. Masking spec v2, sections 7.4, 7.5, 8 and M-33. |

### Module X6 — Later: Data Server app and panels

*Frontend.* The standalone Data Server application and the Editor's panel that reads it. Parked 18 Nov 2026 with module X4.

| Step | Work | Track | Needs | Done when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `19.3` | Standalone Data Server app | UI | 19.2, 11.1 | Its own Tauri 2 executable, installable separately from Editor and Playout. It lists sources, their status, last update, error and sample rows, and also runs headless as a service on a playout machine |
| `19.7` | Editor reads the Data Server | UI | 19.6, 11.19, 8.6 | The Data binding panel browses sources, tables and fields from a running server; binding a property to a server field writes a portable reference, not the value; sample data for preview is a pinned snapshot, so a scene previews offline |
| `19.16` | Data Hub screens built | UI | 19.3, 19.11 | Sources, add a source, adapter editor, simulator and a health board for staleness are built from the Figma screens D1 to D3 plus the simulator. |

## Standing notes

### Render-core acceptance (modules B3 and B5)

- **GPU-first is an acceptance requirement.** Main production drawing, transforms, masks, effects and composition run primarily on the GPU. CPU scene logic and bounded preparation are allowed; CPU full-frame rasterisation or silent fallback cannot qualify. 3.14 reported this gate even where a cached-composition benchmark passed.
- **Colour follows the project (3.15).** This amends ADR-0002 B.1's "render internally in linear, always". 3.3 stays done for what it verified: tagged sources, the two output transforms and the colour ramp. 3.15's done-when was corrected on 13 Oct 2026: as first written it asked for a rendered anti-aliased edge, gradient and blur, which the engine could not draw before 3.17 and 3.22. The overlap is checked as an engine render, and coverage, interpolation and averaging as working-space reference operations the GPU steps must match. **Named gap:** the Editor's Three.js viewport (11.6) does not read the scene's declared space, and which space its blending matches is unmeasured; the engine is the renderer of record, and 13.5 closes the gap unless a measured browser fix lands first.
- **Effects (3.22, 3.25, 11.60 to 11.62).** Every effect and layer style renders on the GPU compositor, ports EffectCraft algorithms with attribution (ADR-0003's reuse catalogue), and may not silently drop an option it cannot render.

### Reopened on 13 October 2026, since closed

Five steps were re-run against their done-when and failed, and went back to active: 3.13 (no silent fallback: non-default trim, object opacity on vector paths and unloadable glTF geometry must render correctly or refuse by name), 3.7 (glTF primitives load their real vertices and the sphere and torus are triangulated grids), 8.1 (every listed tool carries its generated JSON Schema, closed 16 Nov 2026), 8.9 (render.preview answers with an MCP image block for the requested frame) and 8.12 (the editor MCP edit path takes if_revision and an idempotency key through gx-write-semantics, closed 16 Nov 2026). All five are done in the tracker. Steps that needed a reopened step kept their state, because their own done-when was not contradicted: 4.1 and 7.4 (need 3.13) and 11.20 (needs 8.12).

### Open defect: the engine-host test leg does not terminate (found 13 September 2026)

`cargo test -p gx-engine-host` hangs in `the_host_spawns_when_nothing_is_listening` (`services/render-engine/host/tests/ensure.rs`), while the sibling `ensure_refuses_a_reserved_port` passes in the same run: it is the spawn-and-adopt path itself, and the hang survives an external timeout, which points at a spawned gx-render-worker the harness never reaps. Because of it `npm run test:rust` runs `cargo test --workspace --exclude gx-engine-host` so the gate terminates, and `npm run test:rust:host` runs the host leg alone. **That exclusion is a named hole, not a permanent carve-out**: until the hang is fixed, no run of `npm run check` has exercised host supervision, and no claim about it should rest on that gate.

### Design notes carried forward

- **11.25 to 11.28 (13 September 2026, after comparing Viz Artist and Trio, XPression, Singular.live Composer and the CasparCG ecosystem).** 11.25: the contract already names the motion phases (in, out, hold, continue, update) but only presets speak them, so an authored scene could not say where its take-in ends; without it Playout can only cut. 11.26: four tools converged on letting the author declare which properties an operator may drive; 11.19 binds data into properties but nothing declared the outward contract, and invariants 4 and 5 split authoring from taking, so it is load-bearing. 11.27: a filled page must never mutate the template it came from. 11.28: tokens are defaults, a named text style is a saved bundle an author applies, and it resolves tokens rather than copying them, so the two do not become two sources of brand truth.
- **Deliberately not added.** Rundown and playlist authoring belong to Playout, not the design editor. A Graphic Hub-style asset database browser duplicates the services and MCP modules. Viz's full spline editor is excluded by the "do not copy After Effects complexity" rule. In-editor scripting was refused on 13 September 2026 because ADR Part G routes programmatic authoring through MCP, and **reversed on 2 October 2026**: per-scene JS modules run inside 16.4's deterministic sandbox (11.55), while agent authoring still goes through MCP. Live data-source connectors became the Data Server (X4 and X6), because 8.6 makes a data schema discoverable but nothing said where live data comes from.
- **The 2 October 2026 merge (docs/proposals/GrapiX-Editor-Core-and-AI-Readiness-Proposal.md).** 11.36 to 11.42 are foundations, cheap now and expensive to retrofit. Where a proposal item overlapped a done or active step, the new step covers only the missing delta; items overlapping a pending step were folded into it. 8.11's operator-scoped Program tools stay. MaterialKind collapses into one physically lit material (11.50). 2.6's autosave ring is removed (11.43). Refusal-class lint findings block publish and the rest warn (11.42). Only Admin may approve (13.7).
- **Importing from Figma (modules B11 and F5).** "Perfect" is two measurable things on a real corpus: the imported scene renders within the pixel-gate threshold of Figma's own render of the same frame, and the 7.2 compatibility report names every substitution, with zero unnamed ones. No Figma desktop or plugin host is required. Every imported layer stays an ordinary scene object, so every layer is animatable.
- **Layer styles, effects and text animators (12 October 2026).** 11.60 gives Photoshop layer styles the owning step `memory.md` rule 24 said they lacked; 3.22 and 11.62 own the effects library; 11.61 owns text animators. All render on the GPU compositor and port EffectCraft algorithms with attribution.

## Why the order is this order

**Contracts before anything reads them.** Three products developing against each other serialises three teams on whoever's build is broken. Against mock peers and a conformance suite, they move independently (B1).

**The render core and its pixel gate before anything that changes pixels.** Capture the baseline first or the reference is gone permanently (B3, B4). The effects engine sits straight after it because effects and presets are the main thing and every later module draws them (B5).

**Backend before frontend.** Services and MCP exercise the whole domain headlessly, they are the automation story, and they mean the interface is built against an API that is already proven (B6 to B8). The editor core (commands, revisions, the template contract) is cheap now and expensive to retrofit (B9).

**The frontend reads the backend.** The workspace, panels, animation and effects panels read the command model and the engine (F1 to F4); import, scripting, Playout and parity each read the backend module that feeds them (F5 to F7). What is parked (X1 to X6) is independent of all of that and can wait.
